# Invariants — sweep history and feature-area rules

Companion to `CLAUDE.md`. The 10 SACRED biology-correctness invariants live in `CLAUDE.md`; this file holds **everything else** — the known pitfalls [PIT-01]…[PIT-39], the sweep-history entries [INV-36]…[INV-134], persistent settings rules, architecture pointers, conventions, the sister-project note, and the new-feature playbook ("Borrow before respinning").

## How agents should use this file

**Before editing any subsystem, grep this file for the tag.** Each entry carries a `[PIT-NN]` / `[INV-NN]` / `[PREFS]` / `[ARCH]` / `[CONV]` / `[SISTER]` / `[RECIPE]` anchor plus topic keywords so a single `grep` finds it fast.

```bash
grep -n '\[INV-50\]' docs/invariants.md           # by number
grep -n '\[PIT-17\]' docs/invariants.md           # known-pitfall lookup
grep -n '\[RECIPE\]' docs/invariants.md           # new-feature playbook
grep -ni 'master.delete\|wipe' docs/invariants.md # by topic
grep -ni 'synthesis\|protein' docs/invariants.md  # by feature area
```

## Tag index (grep table)

| Tag | Topic keywords |

| `[INV-167]` | **A rename suffix is a CLAIM, and the delete confirm must answer "will I lose this?" — plus the daily snapshot must exist for the BIGGEST library (2026-07-25, from a real data-loss incident).** A cross-collection MOVE landed fragments in a target that already held their names. `_move_copy_commit`'s `_unique_name_for_target` renamed the colliders to `<name> COPY` **purely because the NAME matched** — it never looked at content. All of them held DIFFERENT sequences. The user read "COPY", correctly concluded the app was calling them redundant duplicates, and deleted them. **The app caused the deletion by lying in a name**, and nothing in-app could undo it — library deletes don't enter the undo stack (`undo.empty` ×3 in the log); recovery came from that day's launch snapshot in `snapshots/`. **FIXES.** **(1) Content-aware collision suffix.** Both rename sites (`_move_copy_commit` and `_commit_library_entry_to_collection`) compare CONTENT: " ALT"/" ALT N" when the name matches but the sequence PROVABLY differs, " COPY"/" COPY N" otherwise. Identity comes from the new `_entry_content_key` — `gb_ref` when dehydrated, else a `t:`-prefixed SHA of inline `gb_text`. **`_save_collections` DROPS `gb_ref` for entries carrying inline `gb_text`**, so a ref-only comparison silently never fires in tests — that is why the hash fallback exists. The `t:` prefix is load-bearing: a `gb_ref` blob could itself CONTAIN another entry's `gb_text`, so keys from different namespaces are NOT comparable — `_content_keys_comparable` requires every key non-empty AND same-namespace before a difference counts as proven. **" ALT" needs proof, and so does " COPY"**: the first cut defaulted unknown content to " ALT", which relabelled the duplicate-in-place flow (an entry copied onto ITSELF, provably a copy) as "ALT" and broke 7 tests — mislabelling a real duplicate is its own lie. The notify REPORTS it too: an ALT rename raises the toast to `severity="warning"` ("N marked ALT: same name, DIFFERENT sequence, NOT duplicates"), because the bare "N renamed for name collision" info-toast is exactly what the user acted on before deleting. **(2) The delete confirm answers the real question.** `LibraryDeleteConfirmModal` takes an optional `dup_count` (how many OTHER entries share the content key, counted across EVERY collection — a sequence still referenced elsewhere genuinely survives, and its blob won't be GC'd): 0 → loud red *"This is the ONLY entry holding this sequence"*; >0 → green *"An identical sequence is kept by N other entries"*; `None` (primer library + other non-plasmid callers) → the original neutral wording. Best-effort — a scan failure degrades to neutral rather than blocking the delete (measured 3.9 ms over a 1200-entry library, so it stays on the UI thread). The name is also now `rich.markup.escape`d: the Static renders with `markup=True`, so a plasmid called `pUC19 [old]` had the bracketed part SILENTLY SWALLOWED as a style tag and the dialog asked you to confirm deleting "pUC19" — the wrong name, in the one dialog where that is worst (bracketed text is routine in NCBI-derived names). **(3) The daily snapshot no longer skips the file that matters most.** `_snapshot_data_files` SILENTLY skipped any file over a flat 50 MB `_SNAPSHOT_FILE_SIZE_CAP`, so the largest, most expensive-to-lose library was the ONE file with no daily snapshot — precisely the artifact that rescued this incident. Disk was ALREADY bounded properly by `_SNAPSHOT_TOTAL_SIZE_CAP` (500 MB, oldest-first), so the per-file cap's only remaining job is to refuse a file so large it would evict every other snapshot: it is now `_SNAPSHOT_TOTAL_SIZE_CAP // 2` (pinned by a test so the two can't drift), checked BEFORE the read, and a skip logs a LOUD warning. **Compression was implemented, BENCHMARKED, and REVERTED — do not re-add it here:** `_snapshot_data_files` runs SYNCHRONOUSLY on the launch path, and gzip level 6 measures ~1.2 s per 43 MB of realistic library JSON (~4.5 s for 160 MB, 3.4–3.8× ratio) versus ~0.3 s for a plain copy — exactly the hang the sibling housekeeping pass spawns a daemon thread to avoid. `_prune_old_snapshots` still peels a trailing `.gz` before matching its `$`-anchored date regex (`_dated_stem`), because `Path.stem` of `x-<date>.json.gz` ends in `.json`: such a file would look UNDATED, and undated files are deleted BEFORE any dated one by the aggregate cap — quietly destroying the snapshots a user cared enough to compress by hand. The existence check accepts either form, so a same-day relaunch can't replace a pre-damage snapshot with a post-damage one. **BUG FOUND IN THE OLD TEST:** `test_smoke.py::test_snapshot_skips_oversized_file` asserted `not (snap_dir / "huge-*.json").is_file()` — a LITERAL path containing `*`, which can never exist, so the assertion was VACUOUS and passed while the file was in fact being snapshotted. Now globs properly (and no longer sparse-allocates a cap-sized file). **Tests:** `test_collections.py::TestCopyVsAltRename` (6) + `::TestDeleteConfirmLastCopyWarning` (5, incl. the markup-escape guard and the neutral `None` path) + `::TestDeleteConfirmCountsAcrossCollections` (3); `test_library_bulk_mark.py::TestAltRenameOnMove` (4 — a replay of the incident plus a same-sequence control that must stay a quiet COPY); `test_data_safety.py::TestSnapshotDataFiles::test_oversized_skip_is_loud_not_silent` / `::test_cap_is_half_the_directory_budget` / `::test_no_compression_on_the_launch_path` / `::test_a_hand_compressed_snapshot_counts_as_todays` / `::test_prune_reads_date_through_gz_suffix`. **STILL OPEN (deferred):** library deletes remain outside the undo stack — in-app `Ctrl+Z` cannot bring a deleted plasmid back; recovery is still snapshot/`.bak`-only. |
| `[INV-166]` | **Translation stops being fabricated; ORFs report a real length (2026-07-24, follow-on to [INV-165]).** The two items [INV-165] documented rather than changed, both now FIXED — each was a case of the code answering a question the user didn't ask. **(1) `_translate_cds` no longer force-appends `*`.** Since the initial commit it terminated the result with `*` whenever the last complete codon ISN'T a stop, so a CDS annotated without its stop (the common GenBank case, and every `<1..n` partial) read back as terminated — a phantom answer to "did my construct keep its stop codon?". It also DISAGREED with `_cds_aa_list`, the sibling that feeds the on-screen AA lane + the premature-stop ⚠ count, which never fabricated one. Safe to remove: the sole production caller (`Ctrl+C` on an AA-highlighted CDS) already did `.rstrip("*")`, and the other consumer (`_scrub_cds_protein`, which compares protein before/after a scrub edit) puts BOTH sides through the same function. Output now matches Biopython's `Seq.translate()` exactly — verified over 3000 random CDSes on both strands, plus `codon_start` 1/2/3, `transl_table`, wrap, and IUPAC. Tests: `test_dna_sanity.py::TestTranslateCds::test_no_phantom_stop_is_appended` + `::test_agrees_with_the_aa_lane_translator`. **(2) `_find_orfs` gained `nt_len` + `exceeds_one_lap`.** A circular frame with no in-frame stop for a whole lap yields an ORF LONGER than the molecule, and no `(start, end)` pair on an n-bp circle can express that — the span wrapped back INSIDE the ORF and under-stated it by whole turns (a 486 nt ORF on a 458 bp plasmid drew a 28 bp arc). Reachable whenever **`n % 3 != 0`**, because the reading frame shifts across the origin — NOT the vanishing-rarity case [INV-165] assumed (264 hits in 38 763 ORFs over random 120–400 bp circles). `nt_len` is the exact coding length incl. the stop; `exceeds_one_lap` flags the case and the span is pinned to the near-full circle (`o_e = (o_s-1) % n`), which SUBSUMES the old `o_e == o_s` full-lap nudge. **Consumers updated to read the length, not the coords:** `_synthesis_lint`'s `expect_cds` full-length-ORF check (its coord `_span` was exactly the pattern the field exists to remove — it would have falsely reported "no full-length ORF" for the very ORFs that span everything) and the ORF-table UI, which now shows *(full lap)* instead of *(wrap)*. Additive to the `find-orfs` agent response (V1_GATE-compatible). Tests: `test_dna_sanity.py::TestFindOrfs::test_nt_len_is_reported_and_matches_the_coordinates` / `::test_orf_running_a_full_lap_is_flagged_not_understated` (asserts the case is still GENERATED, so the guard can't rot) / `::test_linear_orfs_never_claim_a_full_lap`. Docs: `?` help gained a Find-ORFs entry (it had none) + the translation-semantics note, README + `docs/features.md` + `docs/agent-api.md` updated. GOTCHA while writing the help: `_HELP_BODY_MD` is a NON-raw triple-quoted string, so a literal `` `Ctrl+\` `` in it is an invalid escape sequence — a `SyntaxWarning` on every import. Keep backslashes out of it. |
| `[INV-165]` | **Full-codebase audit — digest feature slotting, gel band order, primer Tm on ambiguous bases, OT-2 literal emit (2026-07-24).** Fresh from-scratch audit (biology re-derived against brute-force / Biopython references rather than trusting prior sweeps) across biological accuracy, performance, and attack surface. **FIVE fixes.** **(1) `_fragments_from_cuts` emitted INVERTED features on the origin-crossing fragment.** That fragment maps absolute→local MODULARLY, so a slotted piece beginning BEFORE the fragment lands ABOVE `frag_len`; the old `min(new_s, frag_len)` clamp pinned that START to the fragment's END, yielding `start > end`. The inverted feature rode through `_simulate_traditional_cloning` (which shifts start/end by one offset) into the shipped product, where `_feat_len` reads `start > end` as a WRAP — a 49 bp AmpR remnant rendered as a 489 bp "AmpR" on a 600 bp product. Reachable from the standard 2-cut `_excise_fragment_pair` path whenever a vector feature spans the upstream cut. Fixed by resolving an out-of-fragment START to 0 / END to `frag_len`, using the half-open end form `((e-a-1) % n)+1` (the naive `(e-a) % n` sends an end landing exactly on the 3' edge to 0 — the single-cut `a == b` linearisation), and splitting a piece that straddles a LINEAR fragment's own two ends into the two real pieces rather than a meaningless wrap. **(2) `_split_features_at_cuts` slotted by ENDPOINT, losing annotation two ways** — a feature LONGER than one fragment covered the fragments BETWEEN its ends entirely but they got NOTHING (a 230 bp gene over three 100 bp fragments annotated only the two end pieces, middle fragment bare), and on a circular molecule a feature running past EVERY cut lands both endpoints back in the SAME fragment, which endpoint-slotting read as "contained in one fragment". Now slots by SPAN OVERLAP (fragment absolute spans intersected with the feature), with a new `_split="mid"` tag for a fragment wholly inside a feature — added to `_label_disrupted_split_features`'s accepted set too, since a fragment inside a feature was cut on BOTH sides and is disrupted by definition (the recovered piece is the excised lacZ-MCS stuffer itself, which the pre-existing `test_disrupts_feature_with_excised_middle` had been silently missing). **(3) `_agarose_mobility` inverted size ordering at BOTH window boundaries.** The in-window Helling-Goodman-Boyer map spanned the FULL 0..1 lane while the out-of-window branches were squeezed into [0.97, 0.995] / [0.005, 0.03] — the regions OVERLAPPED, so on a 1% gel a 499 bp band scored 0.983 against 500 bp's 1.000 and rendered ABOVE it (and 10 001 bp below 10 000 bp). Visible on any ladder straddling a window edge. Fixed by compressing the in-window map into the interior band `[_GEL_EDGE_BAND, 1-_GEL_EDGE_BAND]` (new const, 0.03) so the reserved edge bands sit strictly outside it, plus `0.5 ** excess` (not `** (1+excess)`) so the extrapolation is CONTINUOUS with the edge instead of jumping half the band one bp outside. **(4) `_pick_binding_region` crashed on any non-ACGT template base.** It caught only `ImportError` around primer3, but `primer3.calc_tm` RAISES `ValueError: Sequence contains non-ACGT base` — and real templates carry them (Plasmidsaurus consensus N at low coverage, NCBI IUPAC ambiguity, the Synthesis composer's IUPAC typing per [INV-53]). `_design_generic_primers` over such a region raised straight out of the worker. Every sibling Tm helper (`_primer_tm`, `_mut_tm`, `_tm_cached`, the `.dna` import `_calc_tm`) already fell back per-call; this one didn't. Fixed with the same fallback, decided ONCE for the whole candidate window (mixing nearest-neighbour and 2+4 estimates across lengths would pick the length whose ESTIMATOR landed nearest the target), ambiguous bases scoring the AT–GC midpoint. **(5) OT-2 `_ot2_literal_errors`** — a custom labware definition is `repr()`-embedded into the generated protocol (correct: `json.dumps` emits `true`/`false`/`null`, not Python), but `repr(float('inf'))` is the bare NAME `inf` and `json.loads` accepts `Infinity`/`NaN` BY DEFAULT, so such a definition compiled to a protocol that NameErrors on the robot. Now refused at validation with a depth cap. **ASSESSED CLEAN — don't re-sweep:** the restriction scanner (300-trial fuzz vs a brute-force IUPAC reference, 10 enzymes × circular/linear — sacred #1/#2/#3/#5/#6 all hold), `_translate_cds` vs Biopython (400 trials, both strands), `_codon_optimize` + all three v1.2.34 passes (`_codon_fix_sites`/`_codon_diversify`/`_codon_fix_gc_window`) round-trip exactly, digest mass-conservation + overhang pairing, `_search_subsequence` (its `end > len(seq)` wrap convention is DOCUMENTED and both consumers handle it — `_circ_slice`/`% total` in primer, `focus_span`'s explicit `raw_end > n` branch in the hub), `_rederive_primer_binding`, XML (`_safe_xml_parse` covers every parse site), zip (bounded streaming read, doesn't trust `file_size`), the agent HTTP server (loopback bind + Host check + `compare_digest` bearer + rate limit + response cap; browser CSRF blocked because the token header forces a preflight the server doesn't answer), BABS network (env-var Ollama host, off-loopback SSRF bounce, hardened opener for HF), no `eval`/`exec`/`pickle`/`shell=True`/bare-except/mutable-default anywhere, all regexes compiled from constants (no ReDoS surface), compute caches all locked + capped. **OT-2 codegen verified NON-INJECTABLE** by AST assertion over hostile strings in every user-controlled field. **PERF: measured, no change warranted** — scan/digest/ORF/search all scale linearly (500 kb: scan 0.73 s cold / 0 ms warm, ORF 0.30 s, exact search 31 ms); a proposed `_iter_match_starts` rewrite onto lookahead-`finditer` was BENCHMARKED and is **2.8× SLOWER** (the zero-width lookahead defeats the literal-prefix fast scan) — **do NOT re-attempt**. **DOCUMENTED, not changed:** `_translate_cds` force-appends `*` when the last codon isn't a stop (display convention since the initial commit, pinned by tests — marks END-OF-FEATURE, NOT a real stop codon; docstring now says so); `_find_orfs` can report a circular ORF whose `length_aa` exceeds what `(start, end)` can express (needs a stop-free frame for a whole lap — read `length_aa`). |
| `[INV-164]` | **Agent write-surface audit — two follow-ups; the multi-save class is otherwise clean (2026-07-21).** A read-only sweep of every `_h_*` agent write handler (19 genuinely multi-store) for the partial-save data-loss class ([INV-159]/[INV-161]) found the surface well-defended — each handler is single-store-per-path, blob-dehydrated-inside-one-save (`_dehydrate_entry` writes+verifies the blob FIRST, a post-metadata orphan is GC'd), delete-then-orphan (benign), or the canonical-then-mirror pattern with pointer-rollback-on-failure ([INV-144]/[INV-161]/[INV-162]). Two small follow-ups shipped: **(1)** `_h_download_plasmidsaurus` now saves with the SYNC library mirror (was `async_sync=True`) — the agent is headless, so deferring the active-collection mirror only opened a crash window where freshly-imported plasmids sat in `plasmid_library.json` but not the active collection, and a crash+restart would rebuild the live file from the collection (`_restore_library_from_active_collection`) and lose them. **(2)** `babs-setup` added to the `_check_deps` no-TUI version-gate exemption ([INV-163]) — it's a pure git-clone/bootstrap CLI, so a stale-Textual box must be able to run it. The `move-part`/`move-experiment` handlers were RE-CONFIRMED to carry only the same same-session live-mirror residual as the reference `move-plasmid`/`move-plasmids` (INV-144) — NOT a new move-primer bug (they DO re-mirror the active side) — accepted as a family-level residual, not re-opened. Tests: `test_smoke.py::TestCheckDepsSubcommandExemption` (babs-setup) + `test_plasmidsaurus_api.py` (import round-trip under the sync mirror). |
| `[INV-163]` | **`splicecraft update` (+ no-TUI subcommands) exempt from the Textual version gate; pipx eager argv must be one token (2026-07-21).** `_check_deps()` runs at MODULE-IMPORT time and `sys.exit(1)`s when Textual is older than `_MIN_TEXTUAL` — but that fired for EVERY invocation, including `splicecraft update`, whose whole job is to REPAIR a stale-Textual install (user report: system Python with distro Textual 8.2.7 → `splicecraft update` blocked by the very gate it exists to fix). Textual imports cleanly at the wrong version; it only fails later in CSS parsing, which the CLI subcommands never reach. Fix: `_check_deps` early-returns — skipping ONLY the version gate, the missing-dependency check still runs — when `sys.argv[1]` is a no-TUI path (`update`, `logs`, `--version`, `-V`, `--help`, `-h`). Companion (caught in the same adversarial review): the pipx eager-upgrade argv MUST be a SINGLE `--pip-args=--upgrade-strategy=eager` token — the two-token `["--pip-args", "--upgrade-strategy=eager"]` form makes real pipx (verified 1.8.0) exit 2 with a usage error (a following token that starts with `--` is parsed as a separate flag), which would have broken the DEFAULT `splicecraft update` on the recommended pipx install. Tests: `test_smoke.py::TestCheckDepsSubcommandExemption` (update/logs/version/help bypass the gate; bare TUI launch still gated) + the corrected `TestUpdateSubcommandCommandBuilder::test_pipx_normal` single-token assertion. |
| `[INV-162]` | **Parts bins + experiment projects get the active-mirror safety library/primers already had (2026-07-21).** Same class as [INV-161], across two more subsystems with a live-file ↔ collection-mirror split (`parts_bin.json`↔`parts_bin_collections.json`, `experiments.json`↔`experiment_projects.json`). Two gaps closed. **(1) No startup restore.** Only library + primers rebuilt their live file from the active collection at launch; parts + experiments did NOT, so ANY pointer↔mirror desync (a set-active whose mirror write failed, or a `move-part`/`move-experiment` whose live re-sync failed) was PERMANENT — it survived relaunch and the next `_save_*` clobbered the active bin/project. Added `_restore_parts_bin_from_active_bin` + `_restore_experiments_from_active_project`, wired into `PlasmidApp.compose()` after `_ensure_default_*` — they mirror the active bin/project's stored contents into the live file on launch, self-healing any desync (and bringing `move-part`/`move-experiment` to the cross-restart-recoverable safety `move-plasmid` already had via the library restore). **(2) No revert on a set-active mirror failure.** `_h_set_active_parts_bin` / `_h_set_active_experiment_project` (agent) + `_switch_active_parts_bin` / the `ExperimentProjectsPickerModal` switch (GUI) durably flushed the active pointer BEFORE the mirror write and, on failure, returned/raised WITHOUT reverting — leaving pointer=NEW over a stale live file (same-session clobber). All four now capture `prev` and revert the pointer (+ re-flush) on mirror failure, matching the plasmid `_h_set_active_collection` + the fixed primer handler. Also corrected a stale comment on the experiment picker switch that wrongly claimed the crash-between-writes case "recovers cleanly" (it clobbered; it now genuinely self-heals via the new startup restore). Tests: `test_parts_bins.py::TestPartsBinMirrorHardening` + `test_experiment_projects.py::TestExperimentMirrorHardening` (restore rebuilds the live file; agent + GUI switch revert the pointer on an injected mirror failure). |
| `[INV-161]` | **Switching the active primer collection must re-mirror `primers.json` — agent + GUI (2026-07-21).** `primers.json` is the live mirror of the active primer collection (`_save_primers` keeps them in lock-step); the active pointer is a separate setting. Setting the pointer WITHOUT re-mirroring left the live library holding the PREVIOUS collection's primers, so the next `create-primer` / save mirrored that stale content back over the newly-active collection and destroyed its primers. The plasmid `set-active-collection` handler + the startup restore always re-mirrored ([INV-83]); four primer-side switch sites did NOT. Fixed: **(agent)** `_h_set_active_primer_collection` now, under `_cache_lock`, re-mirrors `primers.json` from the switched-to collection via `_safe_save_json_mirror` (named only; the `""` default keeps the live library as-is, matching the startup no-op) and REVERTS the active pointer if the mirror write fails. **(GUI)** the three "save primer(s) to a collection" flows — scrub-commit (`_scrub_commit_primers_with_names` caller), designed-primers-commit (`_commit_designed_primers_with_names` caller), and the map-primer `_on_save` — now call `_restore_primers_from_active_primer_collection()` right after the switch (matching the already-correct primer-collection picker sites) so the subsequent `_load_primers()` reads the TARGET collection, not the previous one. Tests: `test_primer_collections.py::TestSetActivePrimerCollectionRemirror` (re-mirror on switch / no-clobber on create / `""` keeps live library / mirror-failure reverts the pointer); the GUI fix reuses the tested `_restore_primers_from_active_primer_collection` primitive + the tested agent path, verified no-regression across the scrub/domesticator/primers suites. |
| `[INV-160]` | **`splicecraft update` upgrades dependencies eagerly by default (2026-07-21).** pip's default `only-if-needed` strategy leaves a satisfying-but-stale dependency in place, so a routine `splicecraft update` could keep running on an old textual/biopython even after the app was bumped. `_build_upgrade_command` gained an `eager: bool = True` param → `--upgrade-strategy eager` for the pip front-ends (pip-user / pip-venv / pip-system / unknown) and `--pip-args=--upgrade-strategy=eager` forwarded to the pip that `pipx upgrade` invokes. uv / pixi re-resolve the whole environment to the highest compatible on every upgrade, so they are already eager (their argv is unchanged). Eager is SKIPPED when pinning (a rollback wants the exact prior state) and opted out with the new `--keep-deps` flag (`splicecraft update --keep-deps` → upgrade only the package). "Latest" stays within SpliceCraft's pins — the biopython `<1.87` cap etc. still hold (pip resolves within the declared constraints; the marker-gated edlib is unaffected). Tests: `test_smoke.py::TestUpdateSubcommandCommandBuilder` (eager default / `--keep-deps` omits it / package stays last / pin-never-eager / pipx `--pip-args`) + `TestUpdateSubcommandFlow::test_keep_deps_flag_runs_plain_upgrade` + the updated pipx exact-argv assertions. |
| `[INV-159]` | **Agent `move-primer` — safe path out of / into the ACTIVE primer collection (2026-07-21).** The live `primers.json` IS the active collection's content (`_save_primers` mirrors one into the other, [INV-50] / PIT-10), so `""` (default) and the active collection's own NAME are the SAME store. `_h_move_primer` now canonicalises any reference to the active collection down to `""` and routes the write through `_save_primers` — the only writer that keeps `primers.json` + the stored mirror consistent. Pre-fix there was NO safe move out of (or into) the active collection: `""` was refused by a mirror-safety guard whenever a named collection was active, and passing the active collection's NAME edited its stored `primers` list DIRECTLY, desyncing it from `primers.json` — a change the next `_save_primers` silently reverted (the primer stayed duplicated in the target). Fix: (1) the catch-22 guard is removed; (2) the omitted-`from` search scans DISTINCT stores only (the active collection is already covered by `""`, so its name is excluded — no spurious "in multiple collections" 409); (3) the store the primer is ADDED to (`to`) is saved BEFORE the one it's removed from, so a crash between the two atomic writes DUPLICATES the primer (recoverable) rather than LOSING it — and when moving INTO the active collection, `colls`' active entry is refreshed from the just-saved (deduped) library so the removal-save keeps `_save_primers`' re-mirror instead of reverting it; the endpoint also emits a `primer.moved` audit log. Tests: `test_agent_api.py::TestPrimerCollectionReadsAndMove` — `test_move_out_of_active_collection` + `test_move_into_active_collection_by_name` (each asserts `primers.json` == the stored mirror after the move), `test_move_to_active_collection_is_noop` (former guard case → correct "already there" 409), and `test_move_{into,out_of}_active_collection_survives_save_failure` (inject an OSError on the second write → the primer is never lost). |
| `[INV-158]` | **Circular plasmid-map image export — SVG + PNG (2026-07-10).** A publication-quality circular map renderer in the pure, app-free sibling **`splicecraft_mapimage`** (L1: imports biology/util/logging L0 only — deliberately NOT `splicecraft_widgets` (L3), so the xterm `_FEATURE_PALETTE` is mirrored inline as `_FALLBACK_PALETTE` + drift-guarded by `test_mapimage.py::test_palette_matches_widgets`; classified in `test_import_layers::_LAYER_RULES` + packaged in BOTH pyproject lists). | `[INV-155]` | **Synthesis paste never extends the upstream feature (2026-07-08).** Both editors' `on_paste` (`SynthesisEditor` DNA + `ProteinEditor`) now call `insert_at_cursor(..., extend_adjacent_feats=False)`. A paste is a NEW chunk, not a continuation of whatever it lands after — with the default `extend_adjacent_feats=True`, a feature whose end == the cursor (e.g. the region you pasted a moment ago, cursor left at its 3′ end) grew by `n_ins` (the `e >= cur and not(e==cur and s==cur)` branch), so pasting one annotated region after another stretched the upstream feature BENEATH the new bases (and the new region's own feature landed inside it → overlap). `False` switches to the `e > cur` test, so a boundary feature (e == cur) stays put while the pasted feats land cleanly in the opened gap. Pasting INSIDE a feature still extends it (`e > cur` holds regardless of the flag) — verified as a regression guard. This matches the earlier `extend_adjacent_feats=False` already used by feature-library Insert / Insert-Site (sweep #17). Tests: `test_synthesis.py::TestSynthesisPasteFeatureIsolation` (DNA paste-after-paste, plain-paste-after-feature, paste-inside-feature, + the protein-motif mirror). |
| `[INV-154]` | **True-switch tab navigation for the workbench menu bar (2026-07-08, follow-on to [INV-153]).** The menu bar (on `SynthesisScreen`/`BabsScreen`/`AutolabScreen` per [INV-153]) previously STACKED screens — clicking another tool pushed it ON TOP of the current workbench, growing the stack without bound. Implemented in the `PlasmidApp.push_screen` override (the single navigation chokepoint): a class tuple `_WORKBENCH_TAB_TYPES` (SynthesisScreen, BabsScreen, AutolabScreen, SimulatorScreen, SequencingScreen, ExperimentsScreen, HistoryScreen, FeatureLibraryScreen, PartsBinModal, PrimerDesignScreen) + `_is_workbench_tab(screen)`; when pushing a tab AND the ACTIVE screen is a tab, it runs the outgoing tab's optional `_on_tab_switch_away()` persist hook then `self.pop_screen()` BEFORE the push (and before the soft-cap check, so a switch is never depth-refused). ModalScreens (Settings/BLAST/Enzymes/Constructor/Mutato) are NOT in the tuple → they still OVERLAY the active tab. **Persistence across the switch:** Synthesis defines `_on_tab_switch_away` → `_save_state_to_app` (the true-switch pops via `pop_screen`, which does NOT run the `dismiss` override, so the hook covers that path); Babs/Autolab need no hook — their state lives in the reused instance (`app._babs_screen`/`_autolab_screen`) that survives the pop, and re-open re-mounts + rehydrates. The per-tab `action_open_*` RESURFACE guards (`if any(isinstance(s, XScreen)…): pop-to-it; return`) still handle re-clicking the SAME tab (they return before `push_screen`, so no needless close+reopen); the true-switch only fires for a switch to a DIFFERENT tab (verified `base→Synthesis` → open Features → `base→Features`, and back restores the Synthesis fragment + lays out). GOTCHA: `_WORKBENCH_TAB_TYPES` is a PlasmidApp CLASS attr referencing the screen classes, so every listed class MUST be defined before `class PlasmidApp` (they all are, < line ~96906). A picker-then-screen flow (Experiments/History) still true-switches correctly because the picker modal is dismissed (popped) BEFORE the tab screen is pushed, so the active screen at push time is the outgoing tab, not the picker. Tests: `test_synthesis.py::TestWorkbenchTrueSwitch`. |

| `[INV-152]` | **Synthesis reopen-hang fix + a UI-stall watchdog (2026-07-08, supersedes [INV-151]'s persistence half).** A user reported the app "hanging" after reopening the Synthesis workbench; the captured UI snapshot showed the pushed `SynthesisScreen` mounted but with EVERY widget at `[x=0,y=0,w=0,h=0]` (never laid out) while the base screen underneath was fine and the snapshot itself (Alt+D) still fired — i.e. NOT a CPU hang but a re-pushed REUSED screen instance ([INV-151]'s `app._synthesis_screen`) that didn't re-layout in a real terminal (headless `run_test` laid it out fine, which is why tests missed it — they asserted data, not `size`). **FIX (1): FRESH screen per open.** `action_open_synthesis` reverts to `push_screen(SynthesisScreen())` (the pre-1.2.7 path, which always lays out); the reused-instance + `_has_mounted_before` + `_restore_editors_after_remount` + the `_dismissed_once`-reset are all removed. Persistence is KEPT via app-level state: `SynthesisScreen.dismiss` (override, still one-shot via super) snapshots DNA (`ed.snapshot()`) + Protein (`pe.snapshot()` + codon_mode) + Operon (`_operon_genes`/`_result`/`_native*` deep-copied) + loaded metadata + active tab into `app._synthesis_state`; `on_mount` calls `_restore_state_from_app()` (via `apply_snapshot`, no Changed/Loaded churn) BEFORE `_seed_undo_baselines()`. Clear (`ed.load("", [])`) empties the editor, and the next dismiss saves the empty state → reopen stays empty. **FIX (2): automatic hang watchdog.** The SIGUSR1 dump was MANUAL; NEW `_HangWatchdog` (module-level `_HANG_WATCHDOG`) is a daemon thread started ONLY by real `main()` (never `run_test`/imports) that watches a heartbeat the App bumps from a `set_interval(_HANG_WATCHDOG_TICK_S=1.0, _HANG_WATCHDOG.tick)` on the UI message-pump; if the beat stalls `_HANG_WATCHDOG_STALL_S=6.0`s it `faulthandler.dump_traceback(all_threads=True)` to the existing `_STACKS_LOG_FD` + logs `event ui.hang_detected`, one dump per stall (re-armed on `tick`). So a future "it hung" report auto-carries the wedged-thread stack. GOTCHAS: the watchdog uses `time.monotonic()` directly (real wall-time, NOT the monkeypatchable `_monotonic`); `_dump` is exception-safe (no FD → just logs). `self.app._synthesis_state` needs `# type: ignore[attr-defined]`. Repro tooling: a `faulthandler.dump_traceback_later` harness driving the exact flow (open→close→reopen→paste the 939 bp AOX1) did NOT reproduce (headless lays out), confirming the real-terminal-only nature. Tests: `test_synthesis.py` — `TestSynthesisPersistence` (now asserts `size.width > 0` on reopen + a fresh instance) / `TestHangWatchdog`. |
| `[INV-151]` | **Synthesis delete-feature-first + screen persistence + a codebase-wide colour-crash fix (2026-07-08, follow-on to [INV-150]).** THREE things. **(1) Delete-feature-first.** `SynthesisEditor` / `ProteinEditor` gained a STATELESS `_feature_matching_selection()` — the index of a feature whose span EXACTLY equals `_user_sel` (which is precisely what a lane-click `_select_feature_span` sets). `delete_at_cursor` checks it FIRST: on a match it removes only the annotation (reassigns `_feats`/`_aa_feats` for the id-cache bust, sets `_merged_sig=None` on the DNA side) and LEAVES `_user_sel` + the bases intact, so a second Delete then removes the highlighted bases via the normal selection path (the match is gone once the feature is deleted). Both the editor `on_key` and the screen fallback `_delete_active_editor_selection` route through `delete_at_cursor`, so it fires regardless of focus. **(2) SynthesisScreen persistence.** `action_open_synthesis` now reuses ONE `app._synthesis_screen` instance (BABS/AUTOLAB pattern — resurface if stacked). Textual REBUILDS the DOM on re-mount, so the editor widgets come back EMPTY; `on_mount` restores their content from the surviving per-tab undo stacks' top (`_dna_undo[-1]` / `_protein_undo[-1]` = current state) via `apply_snapshot` (no Changed/Loaded → no history churn) when `_has_mounted_before`, else seeds the baselines. GOTCHA: `SynthesisScreen` extends `_OneShotDismissScreen`, whose `dismiss()` latches `_dismissed_once=True` FOREVER — so a reused instance must reset `self._dismissed_once = False` on each `on_mount` or the reopened screen can't be closed again. (Sub-tab DNA↔Protein↔Operon switching already persisted — TabbedContent keeps inactive panes mounted.) Clear (`action_new_fragment` → `ed.load("", [])`) stays the explicit per-tab wipe. **(3) Colour-crash hardening [render-wide].** A feature colour that rich can't parse — a 3-digit `#RGB` short hex or garbage from a hand-edited/exported `.gb` — crashes the styled-Text paint with `rich.errors.MissingStyle`/`StyleSyntaxError`. This fires at Textual PAINT time, DOWNSTREAM of every `_build_seq_text` try/except, so no caller guard can catch it; the primary vector was `PlasmidMap._parse` accepting `#`-colours on `len in (4,7)` with NO hex check. NEW module-level `_safe_feature_color(col, fallback)` (cached in `_SAFE_COLOR_CACHE`; validates via `rich.style.Style.parse`, expands `#RGB`→`#RRGGBB`, else → fallback) applied at the FOUR render colour-application points (`_paint_feature_flap`, `_paint_feature_bar`, the label painter, `_build_seq_inputs`) so ANY bad colour renders safely, PLUS `_parse` (both the map parser AND the library-capture parser) now normalises/rejects the qualifier so bad colours don't get stored/round-tripped. Audit (3 read-only agents) also confirmed NO other scroll-double-count sites and NO Ctrl+C crash/wrong-target (the app's `check_action` refuses `copy_selection` on any non-`_default` screen, so `_copy_strand`'s unguarded `query_one("#seq-panel")` never runs there; and `App.query_one` searches the default base screen anyway) — only `AlignmentScreen` Ctrl+C is a benign no-op (no copy handler). | `[INV-150]` | **Synthesis click geometry + copy-paste (2026-07-08, follow-on to [INV-149]).** THREE fixes to the `SynthesisEditor` / `ProteinEditor` from live use. **(1) Scrolled-click double-count.** `_click_to_bp` / `_lane_feature_at` (DNA) + `_click_to_aa` / `_lane_feature_at` (protein) computed the content column as `(screen_x - view.region.x) + scroll_x`. But Textual's `view.region.x` ALREADY includes the horizontal scroll (it goes negative as you pan right — e.g. `region.x = viewport_left - scroll_x`), so the `+ scroll_x` DOUBLE-COUNTED the pan: after scrolling right, clicks resolved past the sequence end and every feature-lane click missed (+ cursor clicks landed at `n`). Fix: `col = screen_x - view.region.x` (no scroll term; `overflow-y:hidden` ⇒ no vertical term either). NB the diagnostic was the UI snapshot showing `Static#syn-view` at `x=-685` while `scroll_x≈687`. **(2) Phantom drag-select off the strand.** `_click_to_bp` / `_click_to_aa` are X-only (row-agnostic, clamp the column), so a click/drag on a feature lane, the 5'/-3' markers' blank pad, or the vertical-centring blank rows still placed a cursor / started a selection. NEW row-aware gates `_dna_row_bp` (returns a bp only when `render_row ∈ {above_rows, above_rows+1}` — the two DNA strand rows; markers sit ON them so a marker click still resolves to bp 0/n) and `_aa_row_idx` (AA strand row = `total_rows`, + the codon DNA row `total_rows+1` in codon mode). `on_mouse_down` + `on_click` use the row-aware gate (no cursor/drag off-strand); `on_mouse_move` keeps the X-only mapping so a drag STARTED on the strand still extends smoothly if the mouse drifts vertically. Lane clicks still highlight the whole feature (resolved first, before the strand gate). **(3) Synthesis Ctrl+C.** The app's `ctrl+c` (`_copy_strand`, `priority=True`) targets the main `#seq-panel`, which is ABSENT under the pushed `SynthesisScreen`, so a Synthesis copy grabbed the wrong/empty selection. NEW `SynthesisScreen` `Binding("ctrl+c", "copy_selection", priority=True)` (screen priority beats the app's priority — verified) → `action_copy_selection` → `_copy_dna_selection` / `_copy_protein_selection`: copies the active editor's `_user_sel` byte-for-byte and stashes the selection's features rebased to `[0,span)` via the shared `PlasmidApp._gather_region_feats` (non-wrap path — the editors are linear) on `app._copied_region` (DNA) / the NEW `app._copied_protein_region` (AA — a SEPARATE stash so a DNA `on_paste` can't match AA letters). `ProteinEditor.insert_at_cursor` gained a `feats=` param (mirrors the DNA one — lays the copied motifs over the inserted span, clipped) and `ProteinEditor.on_paste` now reads `_copied_protein_region` so a protein paste carries motifs. GOTCHAS: `self.app.<PlasmidApp attr>` needs `# type: ignore[attr-defined]` (self.app is typed base `App`) — matches the existing `_gather_region_feats` callsites. Tests: `test_synthesis.py` — `TestSynthesisScrolledClick` / `TestSynthesisRowAwareClicks` / `TestSynthesisCopyPaste`. |

The DNA + protein modal write-back is now the shared static `SynthesisScreen._apply_feature_edit_result(target, result)` (label/type/strand/color/notes + the `SpliceCraft_strand=["double"]` convention + blank-line note split). Protein lane packing was extracted to `ProteinEditor._pack_aa_feats() → (placements, total_rows)` — the SINGLE source shared by the renderer (`_build_protein_lane_text`), `_lane_height`, and `_lane_feature_at`, so the drawn position can't drift from the click target. **(2) R reveals cut sites (DNA tab).** `Binding("r", "toggle_restr_sites")` on `SynthesisScreen` (the DNA editor's keyboard set is ACGTN so a bare `R` bubbles up to it; the protein editor consumes `R` as arginine so it never fires there — the action guards anyway). `SynthesisEditor.toggle_restr_overlay()` scans `self._seq` via `_scan_restriction_sites(..., circular=False, allowed_enzymes=_active_enzyme_allowed_set())` + the persisted `restr_min_len`/`restr_unique_only` — SAME params as the main panel, so R here respects the active enzyme collection. `_display_feats()` merges `self._feats + self._restr_feats` behind an `id()`-keyed sig cache (`_merged_sig`) so `_build_seq_text`'s id-keyed render caches keep hitting; `_refresh_view` lazily re-scans only when `_restr_scan_seq != self._seq` (cursor-only refreshes skip it; the scan is LRU-cached). `_dna_top_row_offset` + `_lane_feature_at` also read `_display_feats()`. **(3) Undo/redo (Ctrl+Z/Ctrl+Y).** Per-tab stacks on `SynthesisScreen` (`_dna_undo/redo`, `_protein_undo/redo`, cap `_UNDO_MAX=250`), TOP = current state. Editors gained `snapshot()` / `apply_snapshot()` (apply does NOT emit Changed/Loaded — guarded by `_undo_suspended` so an undo can't record itself) + a `Loaded` message emitted by `load()`. `@on(Editor.Changed)` pushes a snapshot + clears redo; `@on(Editor.Loaded)` (+ `on_mount` `_seed_undo_baselines`) resets the stack so Ctrl+Z can't cross a document boundary. `action_undo/redo` dispatch on `_active_tab_id()`. **(4) 3′ insert-site fix.** `action_insert_site` now calls `insert_at_cursor(site, extend_adjacent_feats=False)`: with the default (True) a feature ending at the last base EXTENDS over the inserted recognition bases (a loaded CDS filling `[0,n]` swallowed the site at the 3′ end — visible at 5′, invisible at 3′); False keeps the site standalone at either end. **(5) Insert Site stamps a real feature + direction picker.** `action_insert_site` now rides a feature in through `insert_at_cursor`'s feature-rich `feats=[…]` path (rebased to `[0, len(bases))` so it lands exactly over the inserted span, auto-clipped if the length cap truncates): a hot-pink (`_RESTR_SITE_FEATURE_COLOR = "#FF69B4"`, full 6-hex — Rich rejects `#RGB`) `protein_bind` feature labelled for the enzyme, arrowed by the chosen strand. `RestrictionInsertModal` gained a **Direction** `Select` (`_DIR_OPTIONS`, value = the feature strand as a string) and its dismiss payload changed from a bare `str` to `{"enzyme", "strand"}`; `strand == -1` inserts `_rc(site)` (so a directional Type IIS enzyme like BsaI GGTCTC→GAGACC cuts the other way; a palindrome RCs to itself so only the arrow flips). GOTCHAS: `#RGB` short hex is NOT a valid Rich colour — feature dicts need full 6-digit `#RRGGBB` (a `#8c8` test feature crashed the render via `rich.errors.MissingStyle`, which is NOT caught by the editor's narrow `(TypeError,ValueError,IndexError)` guard). The R-scan except is narrowed to `(TypeError,ValueError,KeyError,AttributeError)` (invariant #1 — `test_no_bare_exception_in_synth_editors` forbids `except Exception:` in the editor bodies). Tests: `test_synthesis.py` — `TestSynthesis3pInsertSite` / `TestSynthesisLaneClick` / `TestSynthesisRestrictionOverlay` / `TestSynthesisUndoRedo`. |

Tests: `test_online_lookups.py` (34). |

| `[INV-142]` | **`traditional-clone` circular-insert pick + a codebase-wide type-safety/data-safety sweep (2026-06-30).** THREE things. **(1) `insert_frag_idx` / `insert_circular`:** `traditional-clone` / `simulate-traditional-cloning` only excised the insert as a linear "purified middle fragment", so cutting a cassette OUT of a CIRCULAR plasmid (an Ω multigene → binary vector) silently grabbed the wrong one of its two digest fragments — `vector_frag_idx` existed but there was no insert counterpart. Now `insert_circular=true` digests the insert like the vector (refusing a ≥3-cut), enumerates `(vector_frag × insert_frag × orientation)`, and `insert_frag_idx` picks the cassette (still refuses to guess by size). Verified BYTE-FOR-BYTE against a REAL GoldenBraid Ω→binary build (off-suite, on the user's PRIVATE data — never in the repo): a pCambia-style binary vector cut EcoRI+KpnI drops its reporter cassette + keeps the backbone (KanR + T-DNA LB/RB borders + pVS1 rep machinery), and ligating the Ω's excised multigene reproduced the user's existing binary plasmid exactly (right size, right junctions, reporter gone). Tests: `test_agent_api.py::TestTraditionalCloningCircularInsert`. The `_agent_traditional_cloning_candidates` core is the shared engine; `info` is `(dict|None, err|None)` so add `assert info is not None` after the err-guard. **(2) Type-safety sweep:** the pyright gate's `[tool.pyright] include` listed only the hub + 3 small siblings, so the other ~23 (`splicecraft_agent`/`_babs`/…) were UN-gated and 24 latent `Optional`-handling errors had accumulated (None subscript/iterable, `_save_X(list|None)`, `float(None)`). Fixed all (narrow via `assert … is not None` after a sibling's `(val|None, err|None)` guard; a `_to_float` helper for the babs Ollama-progress coercions) and **expanded `include` to `splicecraft*.py`** so every sibling is now strictly checked (a NEW sibling auto-covered). **(3) Data-safety hole:** `model_collections.json` (BABS, [INV-139]) was in the production `_USER_DATA_FILE_ATTRS` registry but NEVER added to the conftest `_protect_user_data` sandbox list — so babs UI tests read+WROTE the user's REAL `model_collections.json` (a stress-test collection with a long-padded name had leaked into it, and the real 'My Models' count broke `row_count==1` tests once the live Ollama gained models). | `[INV-138]` | **`--agent` auto-headless without a TTY — the durable daemon-spin fix (2026-06-25, follow-up to [INV-137]).** [INV-137] gated the startup MODALS, but the `splicecraft --agent` daemon ALSO pegged a core with no modal up: per-thread CPU pinned it to the **`textual-input`** thread busy-looping. ROOT CAUSE: `--agent` ran the FULL interactive Textual app (`app.run()`); launched detached (stdin = `/dev/null`/pipe, NOT a TTY) the input reader spins on the fd's immediate EOF → 100% CPU, no rendering. (A *blocking* stdin instead HANGS init — Textual queries the terminal on startup; a PTY wrapper `script -qfc` works but is a fragile launch-side workaround.) **FIX:** `main()` forces headless (`app.run(headless=True)` — the existing `--headless` / snag-#3 path, which runs NO input driver) whenever `--agent` is set AND `sys.stdin.isatty()` is false (a detached daemon); an interactive `--agent` on a real terminal still gets the live TUI. The decision is isolated in the pure helper `_agent_headless_for_no_tty(enable_agent_api, stdin_isatty)`. So a plain `setsid splicecraft --agent --no-splash` no longer spins — no PTY and no explicit `--headless` needed. **SACRED: the `--agent` daemon must NEVER run the interactive input driver without a controlling TTY — it busy-loops a core.** Tests: `test_smoke.py -k test_agent_headless_for_no_tty` (the 4-way truth table: daemon-no-tty→headless, interactive-agent→TUI, non-agent→never). |

With no TTY to dismiss them, the modal sits on the screen stack and the render loop spins a core indefinitely — AND the What's New modal RE-ARMS on every restart, because `last_seen_version` only advances in the modal's dismiss callback (never reached headless). What's New: skip the push in headless BUT still `_set_setting("last_seen_version", __version__)` so it can't re-arm/re-spin next launch, then fire any pending update directly. Primer-dup: early-return the whole scan in headless (the safe default is KEEP — never delete user primers without an answerable confirmation). **SACRED: no code path may push a modal (or any screen that needs dismissal) in `--agent`/headless mode — there is no TTY to clear it and the render loop spins a core.** Any NEW startup dialog must gate on `_agent_api_port`/`_headless`. | `[INV-136]` | **Assembly start-codon overlap collapse — no double-Met TUs (2026-06-25).** ROOT CAUSE: the Golden Braid / MoClo CDS fusion overhang **AATG embeds the start codon** (`AATG` = `A` spacer + `ATG` start), and a CDS part body handed to the assembler still carries its OWN leading `ATG` — so `_clone_assembly_into_entry_vector` chaining each junction as a literal `oh5 + body` emitted the `ATG` **twice** (`AATG·ATG·[codon2]`), giving every assembled CDS a spurious extra N-terminal Met (M-M-…). The DOMESTICATION path already collapses this (`_fuse_overhang_body`, the 2026-05-30 fix), so the SAVED L0 part was single-Met — but the ASSEMBLY path never got the same safety net, so the TU/MOD built from it doubled the start. Real-world hit: CDS parts assembled into α/Ω TUs/MODs via the agent `assemble-into-entry-vector` path each gained an extra N-terminal Met — the L0 parts were clean, the assembler injected it (caught by an independent re-audit, corrected by a from-scratch rebuild). **FIX:** the chaining loop collapses the overlap with the SAME predicate as `_fuse_overhang_body` — for a non-first fragment whose oh5 is an AATG-family overhang (`_atg_offset_for_part` > 0: coding type + overhang ends in `ATG`, ≥4 nt) AND whose body starts with `ATG`, drop the body's redundant leading `ATG` and shift THAT fragment's feature coords by the same amount; the AATG start-codon feature extension is suppressed for the collapsed case (the coord shift already lands a body-start feature on the overhang's `ATG`). No-ops for MoClo-Plant's `AGGT` CDS overhang (doesn't embed `ATG`), non-coding parts, UNTYPED parts, and bodies already trimmed to codon 2 — so `test_l0_to_tu_chains_parts_correctly` (untyped CDS) and [INV-135] feature carry are byte-unaffected. **SACRED: an assembled CDS must carry exactly the start codon its L0 part does — the AATG fusion overhang supplies the ATG, the body must NOT double it.** Tests: `test_domesticator.py::TestCloneAssemblyIntoEntryVector::test_l0_cds_with_start_codon_collapses_double_atg` (collapsed junction present + doubled absent; CDS feature extracts the single-Met CDS). |
| `[INV-135]` | **Universal feature carry + history on every cloning step — TUs/MODs surface their real sub-features; Ctrl+S never drops lineage (2026-06-23).** User report: assembled TUs rendered as ONE contiguous block instead of their individual genes, and clonings/mods showed an empty History tab. **Feature carry (the modular GB/MoClo path `_clone_assembly_into_entry_vector`):** (1) `_assembly_fragment_l0` now carries the L0 part's stored `insert_feats` (each sub-feature's label / type / STRAND / colour) onto the assembled TU as the fragment's `features`, so a multi-feature part (domesticated operon, multi-gene block) shows its real genes — with correct strand — instead of the synthesised one-per-part block; falls back to the per-part synth feature only when a part carries no `insert_feats` (single-element parts unchanged). Coords are in `part["sequence"]` frame = the fragment body, so the existing chaining loop tiles them into TU coords (incl. the AATG start-codon extension, already hoisted to cover carried features). (2) **MOD de-dup** — `_assembly_fragment_augment_l0_features` reconstructs whole-part features from `source_parts` for the recursive TU→MOD step, which post-fix-(1) DOUBLES the genuine sub-features riding up from the enriched TU. Fixed by making reconstruction a GAP-FILLER: a recon part-feature is dropped when it OVERLAPS any of the TU's OWN marshalled features (FROZEN before the recon loop, so adjacent parts' few-base AATG-fusion overlaps don't suppress each other — that bug dropped every downstream part on the first pass). Legacy / collapsed TUs (no fine features, or a single whole-insert wrapper the `contained≥2` rule drops) still get every part reconstructed. Mirrors [INV-121] (sub-features REPLACE the whole-part wrapper, never stack). Traditional / Gibson UI / clone-region / PCR / synthetic-FRAG were already clean (5-agent audit); the Golden Gate AGENT endpoint takes raw sequence strings with no input features, so its empty annotation set is honest, not a drop. **(3) Anchor hardening** (so FIX-(1) HAS features to carry): `_best_insert_offset` — the by-sequence anchor `_stamp_insert_feats_on_part` + `_attach_insert_feats_to_record` use to map a part's features onto its (possibly domestication-mutated) insert — gained a 2nd tier. When NO window fits the absolute substitution budget (a heavily but LOCALLY site-cured part — many internal Type IIS cures > budget, separated by long verbatim stretches), a unique-exact k-mer anchor from BOTH ends must AGREE on a constant offset (domestication swaps are same-length / indel-free, so the offset is constant), backstopped by a 50% mismatch-fraction guard; a ±1 indel makes the two ends disagree, a balanced double-indel trips the fraction guard, and an unrelated / mutation-dense insert yields no clean unique anchor — so a heavily-domesticated operon's genes now STAMP + carry instead of fusing into one block, while a feature is STILL never placed at a guessed coordinate (project_primer_design_catastrophic). A 3rd tier handles a FULLY codon-optimized insert (30 %+ rewritten — no clean k-mer survives to anchor): the Domesticator records the EXACT template + region it built the insert from (`_design_worker` → `result["_recoded_src"]`/`_recoded_region`, copied onto the part by `_save`, POPPED at stamp time so it never persists to the bin), and `_recoded_insert_offset` rebases the source features at that KNOWN offset ONLY when the recorded template is BYTE-IDENTICAL to the stamp's source — a no-marker / different / RC / FASTA template, a wrapping region, or a length-changing edit all refuse (the `test_unrelated_lookalike` guard still holds: garbage carries no marker). So a heavily codon-optimized operon keeps its genes via the design-time mapping, never a guess. **History (the universal save contract):** `LibraryPanel.add_entry`'s construction-history logic was extracted to `_apply_entry_construction_history` and now ALSO drives the canvas Ctrl+S worker (`PlasmidApp._save_worker` ← `action_save`), which previously wrote the library entry with NO `history_xml` field — so a built / mutagenized / imported `.dna` plasmid lost its entire lineage on the first Ctrl+S (the "no history on clonings and mods" report; the sync `_do_save`/quit/agent path already went through `add_entry` and was fine). The shared helper preserves prior `history_xml` on a same-id re-save (or consumes a fresh `.dna`'s `_dna_history_xml`), appends an `editSequence` step when the canvas DNA actually changed, and stamps an origin root otherwise. **SACRED: every modular assembly step carries each parent fragment's name/position/STRAND/type to the final plasmid (no whole-part block, no stacked wrapper); every canvas save preserves + extends construction history (Ctrl+S never drops lineage).** Tests: `test_domesticator.py::TestAssemblyFeaturePreservation` (TU sub-features + reverse strand; back-compat synth feature; MOD all-genes-no-dup; augment gap-fill for collapsed/legacy), `test_origin_history.py::TestOriginHistoryIntegration` (canvas Ctrl+S preserves + appends history; no-edit keeps prior verbatim), `test_domesticator.py::TestFragmentSourceFeatures` (heavy-local-repair anchors; indel / mutation-dense / recurring-region refused; heavy-domesticated part still stamps its genes), `test_clone_handoff.py::TestStampInsertFeatsAnchoring` (codon-optimized part rebases via byte-identical design provenance; no-marker / different-template / length-change / wrap all refused). |
| `[INV-134]` | **Windows console UTF-8 + live ASCII-map toggle — fix the "garbled mess" (2026-06-14).** Users reported the braille plasmid map rendering as mojibake on Windows cmd / even Windows Terminal on Win 11. ROOT CAUSE: a Windows console's OUTPUT code page defaults to the legacy OEM page (437/850/932/…), so the map's UTF-8 braille bytes (U+2800-U+28FF) get decoded as that page → garble. (Python's `print` dodges it via the Unicode `WriteConsoleW` API; a TUI's byte-level output does not.) **Primary fix — `_windows_enable_utf8_console()`**, called as the FIRST thing in `main()` (before banner / splash / TUI): `SetConsoleOutputCP/SetConsoleCP(65001)` (the programmatic `chcp 65001`) + enables ENABLE_VIRTUAL_TERMINAL_PROCESSING (0x0004) on STD_OUTPUT_HANDLE so ANSI escapes aren't printed literally. Uses `ctypes.windll.kernel32`; best-effort (every quirk caught — must NEVER block launch); reversible (previous code page restored via `atexit`); no-op off Windows. Surfaced as `win_utf8_console` in the `startup.terminal_capabilities` event. **SACRED: never auto-downscale a CAPABLE terminal to ASCII** — `_select_render_tier` keeps braille whenever UTF-8 is OK and no env force (the user's complaint was the opposite of over-downscaling). **Secondary fix — live, persisted ASCII toggle** for the residual FONT-tofu case (UTF-8 fine but the font lacks braille glyphs, e.g. legacy conhost Consolas): `_set_ascii_mode()` flips the module `_ASCII_MODE`; `PlasmidApp.action_toggle_ascii_map` + the Settings → Display checkbox `#set-ascii-map` persist `ascii_map`, bust `PlasmidMap._draw_cache`, and repaint. `_ASCII_MODE` is now part of the `PlasmidMap` draw-cache KEY so the live flip can't serve a stale render. (1) **Amplicon 3′-end bug (CATASTROPHIC-CLASS):** the cured amplicon was `fwd_tail + body + rev_tail` — the reverse-primer 5′ tail (`pad+site+spacer+_rc(oh3)`) appended VERBATIM, so the saved PCR fragment didn't end in `_rc(reverse_primer)` and its 3′ Esp3I site pointed the wrong way (user: "3′ end shows a reversal of the 5′ end of the reverse primer"). The SOE design (`_design_operon_soe_primers`, primer = `rev_tail + rev_bind`) was already correct; only the reconstruction was wrong. (2) **Primer-family naming:** `NamePlasmidModal` gained an opt-in `primer_family=` → a "Primer Family Name" box (dismiss dict carries `"primer_family"`); `_native_save_outputs(primer_family=…)` rebases the auto `operon-DOM-#-F/R` names to `{family}-DOM-#-F/R` via a local `_fam()` (plain prefix-swap, not regex) across run-primers, amplify-history oligos, AND the primer-library entries — one family renames the whole set everywhere. (3) **Source-plasmid annotation:** the lift now records the source library id (`_native_set_operon(source_id=…)`, passed from the canvas lift's active `#library` id + the plasmid-picker's entry id; None for NCBI); the save writes the family-named SOE primers back onto that source entry — `_load_library` → `_attach_named_primers_to_record(src_rec, run_primers)` (re-derived by sequence; the cure shows as a mismatch; **dedups against existing primer_seqs so re-domestication never piles up**) → `_save_library(async_sync=True)` (reseats cache + mirrors active collection). Best-effort, library-sourced only. (4) **Post-save focus:** `_native_focus_saved_clone` pops the workbench, `_apply_record`s the clone onto the canvas, repopulates the library, and `move_cursor`s to the saved row by name→id; best-effort (own try/except, never sinks the save). (5) **No underscores ([INV-98]):** amplicon `rec._tui_display_name = pcr_name`; the clone already gets it from `_part_to_cloned_seqrecord` — so the chosen names render verbatim, never the sanitised LOCUS. **SACRED: a tailed-primer amplicon's 3′ end is `_rc(rev_primer_tail)`, never the tail verbatim; the source write-back goes through `_save_library` (backed-up) and dedups by primer_seq; post-save focus is best-effort and never raises into the save.** Tests: `test_operon_ui.py` (amplicon 3′ == `_rc(rev)`, `{family}-DOM-` rebase, source plasmid annotated, post-save focus loads the clone, no-underscore display name). |

| `[INV-113]` | **Cross-platform native clipboard tier (2026-06-09).** `_copy_to_clipboard_with_fallback` relied on OSC 52 (Textual tier-1 + manual tier-2), which is FIRE-AND-FORGET: a terminal that ignores it (GNOME Terminal / VTE on Wayland, an oversized payload, `tmux` without passthrough) makes the copy LOOK successful while the system clipboard stays empty — and because Textual's `copy_to_clipboard` never raises, the chain stopped at tier-1 and the file fallback never fired (user report: F9 readout absent from the Wayland clipboard, zero tier-3 files written). **Fix = a new tier-0 native tool** (`_copy_via_native_tool` → `_clipboard_native_commands()`): session-appropriate WRITE tools that actually set the clipboard AND report success via exit code — macOS `pbcopy`; Windows `clip` then PowerShell `Set-Clipboard`; WSL `clip.exe` then `powershell.exe` (Linux tools have no display there); Linux/BSD Wayland `wl-copy`; Linux/BSD X11 `xclip` then `xsel`; Android `termux-clipboard-set`. **Preferred over OSC 52** because it's verifiable; **skipped when nothing applies** (headless / SSH remote: no `DISPLAY`/`WAYLAND_DISPLAY`) so OSC 52 — the correct mechanism for a remote terminal — still runs. Each candidate is `shutil.which`-gated, fed text on stdin as UTF-8, `stdout`/`stderr` discarded (xclip/wl-copy fork to hold the selection), non-zero/timeout/OSError falls to the next. `_is_wsl()` is computed FRESH (not cached) so tests can monkeypatch the environment per-case. New return mode `"native"`; `_notify_copy_outcome` AND F9 `action_capture_ui_snapshot` now treat `native`/`clipboard`/`osc52` as success (the snapshot check previously tested a dead `"textual"` mode that the helper never returns). Structured event `clipboard.copy {tier, tool?, chars}` on every copy so the used tier is diagnosable from a log paste. **SACRED: native-first but only when a tool applies; NEVER log clipboard TEXT — only tier / tool / char-count (`[INV-38]`); tests MUST mock `_copy_via_native_tool` / `_clipboard_native_commands` rather than assume the runner's installed tools (every existing fallback test disables the native tier).** Docs: `docs/PLATFORMS.md`. | `[INV-112]` | **`splicecraft update` post-upgrade version verification (2026-06-09, apply-side companion to `[INV-111]`).** Exit 0 from the upgrade front-end (pipx/pip/uv/pixi) is NOT proof the version changed. Right after a release, PyPI's Simple-API index (what pip/pipx resolve against) lags its JSON metadata (what our CHECK reads) by a minute or two, so the check sees the new version, runs `pipx upgrade`, and pipx — still seeing the old version as latest — no-ops and exits 0. The old code trusted that exit and printed "Upgrade complete." over an UNCHANGED install (user report: "confirmed but still on the old version"). **Fix in `_update_run_install`:** after a 0 exit, re-read the ACTUALLY-installed version via the new `_query_installed_version()` — a FRESH `sys.executable` subprocess (`importlib.metadata.version('splicecraft')`), NOT the running process's `__version__` (that's the stale OLD code) and NOT an in-process metadata read (can be stale after pip swaps the dist-info underneath us); `sys.executable` is the install's own interpreter so it sees exactly the env the upgrade touched. **Reach logic:** a PIN must match EXACTLY (precise request, incl. downgrades); a plain upgrade is satisfied by installed ≥ target (PyPI may have moved on to an even newer release between check and resolve); unparseable either side falls back to exact string compare. **On not-reached → honest failure (exit 1):** no-change-at-all (installed == running `__version__`) prints the post-release-skew message ("still on X — PyPI may still be propagating; wait a minute and re-run; or `--force`"); changed-but-wrong prints the raw installed-vs-expected. **Unverifiable readback (None) → exit 0 + "couldn't confirm"** (never claim a success we can't confirm, never hard-fail a command that itself succeeded). Events: `update.install.verified` / `.version_mismatch` / `.unverified`. **SACRED: never reroute the install-success report to trust the subprocess exit code alone — always verify the installed version; in tests, MOCK `_query_installed_version` (every happy-path flow test does) rather than letting it shell out.** Also folded in (test-only): `tests/test_codon.py::_settle_tab` now requires the active tab to HOLD across two drains — a trailing `TabActivated` from a prior switch could land after a premature first match and revert it, flaking `test_fetch_done_adds_and_selects` on the loaded 2-core CI runner (`'sp-tab-fetch' == 'sp-tab-library'`) and sending spurious "Tests failed" emails after clean releases (publish itself was always fine — `publish.yml` is a separate, tag-triggered workflow). Tests: `test_smoke.py::TestUpdateSubcommandFlow` (no-op→exit 1, verified-success, unverified, pin-exact-mismatch) + `_query_installed_version` unit cases; `test_cross_platform.py::test_update_install_passes_list_not_shell` mocks the readback. |
| `[INV-111]` | **PyPI update-check self-heal + honest failure reasons (2026-06-09, follow-up to the v1.0.37 cap fix `4ceaccb`).** Root-cause recap: `/pypi/splicecraft/json` embeds the full per-file `releases` history (~94% of the blob, ~1.5 KB/release); `info.version` sits at byte ~25.9 K, immediately BEFORE `releases` at ~26.0 K. The v1.0.37 fix raised the response cap 256 KB→8 MiB, but a hard cap on an ever-growing document is a deadline, and — worse — EVERY fetch failure (network, oversize, parse) collapsed to `None` and printed "Could not reach PyPI", sending users to debug a network that was fine. **Three mechanisms (`_fetch_latest_pypi_version_ex`).** (1) **Honest reasons:** `_fetch_latest_pypi_version_ex() → (version, reason)`, `reason ∈ {_PYPI_FETCH_OK, _NETWORK, _OVERSIZE, _PARSE, _SCHEMA}`; `cmd_update` prints a DISTINCT message per reason and states "PyPI itself is reachable" for the non-network cases — **only `_NETWORK` may print "Could not reach PyPI"** (locked by `test_pypi_oversize_reports_distinct_message`). (2) **Self-heal:** an oversized OR unparseable full response no longer gives up — `_extract_version_from_pypi_head(raw[:_PYPI_HEAD_READ_BYTES])` (64 KiB) recovers `info.version` from the bounded head, which is IMMUNE to release-history growth (the head always clears `version` before the unbounded `releases`). The extractor (`_PYPI_HEAD_VERSION_RE`) scans ONLY the slice BEFORE the first `"releases"` marker, so a future per-file `version` key can never be mistaken for the package version (`test_head_extract_ignores_version_after_releases`). The 8 MiB cap is now a pure DoS guard with this as the backstop. (3) **Early-warning telemetry:** `update.probe.fetched {response_bytes, pct_of_cap}` on every success, `update.probe.recovered_from_head` on recovery, and the in-app worker logs the TRUE reason (was the opaque `"pypi_fetch_returned_none"`) — so metadata growth toward the cap shows as a trend years ahead. **SACRED:** `_fetch_latest_pypi_version(*, reason_out=None) → str-or-None` stays the stable, widely-monkeypatched entry point — NEVER reroute callers to the `_ex` form (it breaks every test that patches the wrapper); pass `reason_out=[]` to capture the reason. Builds on `[INV-36]` (`$SPLICECRAFT_PYPI_URL` override still uses the JSON path) + `[INV-37]` (1-retry / 250 ms network policy) + `[PIT-20]` (network-read size cap). Tests: `test_smoke.py` — `TestShiftClickFeatureExtend` (reason per failure mode, both self-heal paths, head-extractor anchor / empty / garbage, `reason_out`, growth-rate headroom) + `TestUpdateSubcommandFlow::test_pypi_oversize_reports_distinct_message`. |

| `[INV-108]` | **Pure-Python RBS / translation-initiation engine (2026-06-06, follow-up to `[INV-107]`).** Three `splicecraft_biology` functions on top of the validated RNA folder: **`_rna_cofold(A, B)`** — bound-state heterodimer ΔG (e.g. the 16S anti-SD : mRNA hybrid); **`_rbs_strength(mrna, start_pos)`** — relative E. coli translation-initiation strength (forward); **`_rbs_design(cds, target_strength)`** — reverse-design a 5'UTR (SD + spacer) to a target (search a graded SD×spacer ladder, score, pick closest). Exposed read-only via the `cofold-rna` / `rbs-strength` / `design-rbs` agent endpoints. **Two-tier validation, honestly bounded:** the STRUCTURAL energies are EXACT (`_rna_cofold` matches ViennaRNA RNAcofold to the cent on all binding/anti-SD duplexes; cofold = the single-strand engine + DuplexInit `+410` in the exterior + a junction term `mm_ext[pairtype(s[j],s[i])][s[j-1]][s[i+1]]` (reversed/inward) replacing the hairpin for cut-spanning pairs; intra-strand structure inside the duplex is forbidden = the constrained bound state — the ~1% diffs vs unconstrained RNAcofold are where the ribosome would melt mRNA structure). But the TIR has **NO thermodynamic oracle** — `ΔG_total = best-register hybrid + ΔG_start + ΔG_spacing − ΔG_mRNA`, `rel_strength ∝ exp(−β·ΔG_total)`, where **β=0.45, the start-codon ΔG lookup, and the spacing-penalty curve are literature-standard CALIBRATION constants, not first-principles** (user chose first-principles + relative-ranking, NO external expression dataset — `[project_operon_rbs_tool]`). So **only RATIOS are meaningful** (a ranking score for tuning, NOT an absolute rate). Validated by RELATIVE RANKING on every canonical determinant: SD strength (strong>medium>weak), start codon (AUG>GUG), spacing (optimum beats too-short AND too-long, short penalised harder = steric), 5'UTR occlusion (SD buried in a hairpin → ~185× weaker), and `_rbs_design`→`_rbs_strength` round-trips exactly. **Hardened:** ACGU(T)-only, empty/ambiguous/out-of-range/non-string/bool all raise `ValueError`; start-too-close-to-5'-end → strength 0; cofold combined-length cap (`_RNA_COFOLD_MAX_LEN = 400`); CDS must be ≥3 nt (its first codon sets ΔG_start — a non-canonical first codon scores ΔG_start=0, it is NOT rejected); `target_strength` must be finite (audit 2026-06-07). Standby is folded into the windowed `ΔG_mRNA` (no separate term). NO attribution to any specific published model (`[feedback_no-salis-attribution]`) — generic biophysics only. `_rbs_design` runs ~80 fold/cofold evals (~seconds; a coarse-to-fine speedup is a noted future refinement). Tests: `tests/test_rna_fold.py::TestRnaCofold` (frozen RNAcofold reference) `::TestRbsStrength` `::TestRbsDesign` + `test_agent_api.py::{TestCofoldRnaHandler,TestRbsStrengthHandler,TestDesignRbsHandler}`. |
| `[INV-107]` | **Pure-Python RNA secondary-structure free-energy engine (2026-06-06).** `splicecraft_biology._rna_fold(seq) → (dot_bracket, dg_kcal)`, plus `_rna_mfe` and `_rna_eval_structure` — a dependency-free Turner-2004 nearest-neighbor MFE folder + structure evaluator reproducing the ViennaRNA **dangles=2** model EXACTLY. **Validated term-by-term against ViennaRNA** (a dev/validation oracle ONLY — never a shipped dependency): the evaluator matches **3705/3705** real folded structures to 0.01 kcal/mol across every loop type (stacks; hairpins incl. special tri/tetra/hexaloops + terminal mismatch + terminal-AU; bulges; internal loops incl. the int11/21/22 special tables; multiloops; exterior dangles), and the Zuker MFE folder matches ViennaRNA's MFE on **551/552** sequences (the lone "miss" is the empty string, where ViennaRNA returns a 100000 sentinel and the folder returns 0.0) with **0 co-optimality violations** + **0 self-inconsistencies** (the returned structure always evaluates to the reported MFE). Parameters are the standard Turner-2004 set, embedded as a **gzip+base64 constant** (~32 KB) so nothing is read from disk and no compiled RNA library is required; `DEF` cells resolve to a uniform **−0.50 kcal** default (MEASURED against the oracle — the GU/UG wobble stacks, every mismatch/dangle table, and the 29 DEF cells of int22). | `[INV-106]` | **Built-in pure-Python Myers/Hirschberg global aligner (2026-06-06, follow-up to `[INV-91]`).** `_pairwise_align`'s **global** mode is now a THREE-tier cascade: **edlib** (turbo, when its wheel is installed) → the new built-in **`_myers_align_global`** (no dependency, every platform/Python) → **Biopython** (the ultimate per-call safety net + local mode). Myers REPLACES Biopython as the universal fallback, so the platforms `[INV-91]`'s marker leaves without an edlib wheel — Linux-aarch64, Python ≥3.14, native Windows, every fresh `pipx install` — go from the ~8.6 s Biopython DP to **~0.7 s** on a real ~18 kb / ~99%-identity Plasmidsaurus read (**~12×**; edlib still ~0.02 s). Each tier is **round-trip-guarded** (the ungapped rows must reconstruct the normalised inputs) and cascades to the next on any failure, so a defect in Myers can never reach the QC counts (Biopython remains the universal net, `[INV-91]`). **Why it's fast enough in pure Python:** Python's arbitrary-precision ints give the Myers bit-vector for free (the whole pattern is one int — no 64-bit word-blocking edlib's C needs), and an IUPAC-aware longest-common-prefix/suffix trim applied at EVERY Hirschberg level peels the long exact-match runs between scattered sequencing errors, collapsing the work (the single biggest lever — 1.95 s → 0.70 s on the scattered-SNP case). Components: `_myers_edit_profile(pattern, text, peq)` (bit-vector edit-distance profile; the `ph |= 1` boundary is the GLOBAL-alignment variant, not approximate-search) · `_myers_dp_global` (small-block Needleman–Wunsch with traceback — the Hirschberg base case below `_MYERS_DP_AREA = 4096` cells; tie-break diag > gap-in-target > gap-in-query, reproducing edlib's gap placement on unique-optimal alignments) · `_myers_align_core` (recursive-trim + Hirschberg split; `a` strictly shrinks each call so recursion always terminates, depth O(log len) ≈ 18 at the 200 kb cap) · `_myers_build_peq` + `_MYERS_COMPAT_CHARS` (per-pattern-char IUPAC `peq` bit-vectors built ONCE from `_iupac_compatible` — the SAME predicate `_pairwise_align` counts with and edlib gets via `additionalEqualities`, so every engine and the counting agree on ambiguity). **Correctness is EXACT** — validated against a naive O(nm) DP for round-trip + minimum-edit-distance OPTIMALITY + IUPAC over thousands of random pairs; counts are IDENTICAL to edlib on any unique-optimal alignment (verified `(1996, 1, 3)` on the 1-SNP-+-3 bp-deletion case), and on divergent reads the engines pick different CO-optimal alignments (same edit distance) — immaterial, those reads are discarded by `_pick_best_rotation`, exactly as `[INV-91]` notes. **Packaging (user choice 2026-06-06):** edlib STAYS marker-gated auto-install in `dependencies` (zero install change; x86_64/macOS<3.14 keep the turbo automatically) — it's now a pure TURBO over a capable built-in, non-load-bearing, not the difference between fast and unusably slow; `scripts/check_dep_wheels.py` unchanged. `_log_startup_banner` now records `edlib : … (active — turbo aligner)` / `not installed — built-in Myers aligner`. Tests: `TestMyersAligner` (7 — optimal+round-trip-vs-naive over 400 random IUPAC pairs, profile-matches-naive-prefixes, DP-base-case-optimal, identical-fast-path, pure-insertion/deletion, recursive-trim-scattered-optimal, length-cap) + `TestPairwiseAlignEngine` (+6 — Myers-invoked-when-edlib-absent, Myers-round-trip-guard-falls-back, Myers-exception-falls-back, `_myers_align_global`-round-trip, IUPAC-as-match, Myers≡Biopython≡edlib on the controlled unique-optimal pair). |

| `[INV-102]` | **Content-addressed plasmid blob store (v1.0.23, 2026-06-03).** De-duplicates `gb_text` (the bulk of every entry) across the library + every collection. Each unique `gb_text` is stored ONCE as an immutable write-once blob at `<DATA_DIR>/plasmid_blobs/<sha256>.gb`; entries carry a small `gb_ref` instead of inline text. **Design = minimal blast radius:** caches stay fully MATERIALISED (every existing reader still sees inline `gb_text`) — only the on-disk form + the save/load boundary change. **Primitives** (`_blob_hash`/`_blob_path`/`_blob_write`/`_blob_read`/`_blob_exists`): `_blob_path` validates the ref as 64 lowercase hex (path-traversal guard); `_blob_write` is idempotent (name==hash ⇒ skip), atomic (`_atomic_write_bytes`), and re-hashes the landed bytes (unlink+raise on mismatch); `_blob_read` re-hashes and returns None (loud) on missing/corrupt/non-utf8 — NEVER a silently-wrong/empty sequence. **Dehydrate/rehydrate** (`_dehydrate_entry`/`_rehydrate_entry` + `_entries`/`_collections` variants): inline `gb_text` ALWAYS wins over a stray `gb_ref`; all return NEW dicts (no mutation, [PIT-17]); a missing blob on rehydrate leaves `gb_text=""` but RETAINS the ref (auto-resolves if the blob is restored). **Activation = single chokepoints:** dehydration lives INSIDE `_safe_save_json`, path-gated on `_LIBRARY_FILE` (`_dehydrate_entries`) / `_COLLECTIONS_FILE` (`_dehydrate_collections`) so EVERY save path is covered present+future (in-place workers, Restore, mirror, agent); blobs are written+verified BEFORE the metadata write so a failure ABORTS rather than reference a missing blob; entry COUNT is preserved so the shrink guard still sees true counts. Rehydration lives in the two cache-populate funcs (`_ensure_library/collections_cache_populated_and_migrated`) — the only load callsites — so `_safe_load_json` stays generic. Migration is automatic on first save; old inline files read unchanged (2-version backward compat). **Orphan GC** (`_gc_orphan_blobs`, startup housekeeping): QUARANTINES orphans to `plasmid_blobs/.orphaned/` (30-day retention, mtime re-stamped), NEVER hard-deletes; ABORTS if any current metadata file is unreadable; treats refs in retained BACKUPS as live; skips blobs younger than `_BLOB_GC_GRACE_SECONDS` (1 h — protects in-flight saves). `_PLASMID_BLOB_DIR` is in `_USER_DATA_DIR_ATTRS` ⇒ included in the pre-update snapshot + wiped by Master Delete (conftest monkeypatches it per-test). **SACRED: blobs are immutable + verified; never trust an unverified blob, never hard-delete one, never let a save reference a blob it didn't first persist+verify.** Tests: `tests/test_blob_store.py` (primitives, dehydrate/rehydrate, integration, GC). |
| `[INV-101]` | **Data-dir disk-hygiene + integrity sweep (2026-06-03).** Six coordinated changes to the sacred `_safe_save_json` / `_safe_load_json` path; all carry tests in `tests/test_data_safety.py`. **(1) Mirror-swap shrink suppression** — `_MIRROR_SWAP_TOKEN` + `_expected_mirror_swap()`; the active-collection switch now routes through `_switch_active_collection_library` (grep it to enumerate the 6 GUI + 1 agent switch sites: collection create×2, delete-promote, click-through, Load button, align-result, `set-active-collection`), and `_safe_save_json_mirror` arms the same token instead of `_allow_catastrophic_shrink`. When the token is positive the shrink guard LOGS but skips BOTH the `lost_entries/` spill AND the >90% catastrophic refusal — the dropped entries live in the sibling `collections.json`/`*_collections.json`, so the spill was pure waste (a ~150 MB write per collection switch = the 1.5 GB `lost_entries/` residue) and the refusal was a latent bug (big→tiny switch raised RuntimeError). **Distinct from `_allow_catastrophic_shrink`, which still KEEPS the spill** (Restore-from-backup is genuinely destructive). Don't blanket-wrap `_save_library` in the token — only the switch callsites; the general edit/add/delete path must keep full guard protection. **(2) `lost_entries/` pruning** — `_prune_lost_entries` (count 5 + 500 MB cap, never below 1); pre-fix nothing pruned it.
