* **Sacred — cap + IUPAC-only typing.** `_SYNTHESIS_MAX_BP = 50_000` (generous headroom above the ~30 kb commercial-synthesis ceiling so `_build_seq_text` stays interactive on a single chunk). `_SYNTHESIS_TYPEABLE_BASES = frozenset("ACGTRYWSMKBDHVN")` mirrors `AddFeatureModal`'s validation set so a fragment composed here round-trips through the feature library without surprises. `insert_at_cursor` truncates at the cap with a notify; non-IUPAC chars dropped with a notify.
* **Sacred — feature-shift on insert/delete.** Insert at cursor: bases right of cursor shift right; features whose `start >= cur` shift; features whose `end >= cur` shift, with the carve-out that `end == cur && start == cur` (zero-length features parked at the cursor) DON'T extend. A feature whose `end == cur && start < cur` extends (the typical "append to upstream feature" case). Delete: features entirely left of deletion unchanged; entirely right shift by `n_del`; overlapping clipped; zero-survival drops. `_delete_range` is the canonical mutation primitive — `delete_at_cursor` (Backspace / Delete / selection-delete) routes through it.
* **Two message classes.** `SynthesisEditor.Changed` fires on mutation (sets `_dirty = True`, refreshes status); `SynthesisEditor.CursorMoved` fires on cursor / selection change (refreshes status only, NO dirty flag). Screen listeners use `@on(SynthesisEditor.Changed)` / `@on(SynthesisEditor.CursorMoved)` — NEVER `@on(Message)` (catches every bubbling message, polluting handler scope).
* **Feature library side panel.** Right-pane DataTable populated from `_load_features()` with two action modes: *Insert* splices the entry's sequence at the cursor AND annotates it (reverse-strand entries get `_rc`'d so the annotation matches the template strand); *Annotate* overlays the entry's type / colour / qualifiers onto the current selection without changing DNA (notifies if no selection). `_featlib_rows` cache is `list[(row_key, entry_dict)]` where row_key is `f"{name}|{ftype}|{hash(seq):06x}"` so dup names with different sequences resolve uniquely.
* **Restriction-site picker.** `RestrictionInsertModal` is a searchable picker over `_NEB_ENZYMES`; dismiss payload is the enzyme name (caller resolves the recognition site via `_site_for_enzyme` and `insert_at_cursor`s it at the cursor — IUPAC degeneracy preserved, not expanded, so a vendor handles ambiguous bases per their own policy).
* **AddFeatureModal `total_len` kwarg.** New optional keyword (default None) lets callers running outside the main app (like SynthesisScreen, which doesn't host `#seq-panel`) supply the length used by the CDS divisibility-by-3 check. None falls back to the live `#seq-panel` query — backwards compatible with the on-canvas Ctrl+F path.
* **`_blocks_undo: bool = True`** on `SynthesisScreen`, `SynthesisLoadModal`, `RestrictionInsertModal`, `SynthesisUnsavedChangesModal` — every one mutates record state or hosts an Input. Attr placement after docstring per sweep #6 (`[INV-41]`).
* **Logging events:** `synthesis.*` (new / load / save / rename / feature.add / featlib.insert / featlib.annotate / insert_site). The `app.open.synthesis` action-log fires from `action_open_synthesis` via `@_action_log`.

## [INV-54] Synthesis composer DNA/Protein tabs (2026-05-20)
`SynthesisScreen` is a two-tab workbench (TabbedContent: DNA + Protein) sharing one toolbar. Actions dispatch on the active tab via `_active_tab_id()`. Tested by `tests/test_synthesis.py` (80 cases). Sacred invariants:
* **Per-tab state isolation.** Each tab carries its own `_loaded_id` / `_loaded_name` / `_dirty` / `_saved_snapshot` — DNA tab uses the bare names (back-compat with the v0.9.x single-tab handlers), protein tab carries explicit `_protein_*` variants. Save / Load / Rename / New all dispatch through `_active_tab_id()`. Tab switch fires `TabbedContent.TabActivated` → `_refresh_status()` re-renders the status line for the now-active tab. Close prompts unsaved-changes when EITHER tab is dirty (the modal's "save" decision saves the active tab; "abandon" drops both).
* **`ProteinEditor` mirrors `SynthesisEditor` semantics in AA coords.** `_aa_seq` (single-letter codes, `*` for stop), `_cursor_pos` on AA[N] (reverse-video paint). Direction-aware anchor for shift+arrow (same fix as DNA — first shift+left includes the AA under the cursor). Two render modes selected by `_codon_mode`: **codon-translated** (2 rows: AA centred over 3-bp codon; `_cols_per_aa() = 3`) and **AA-only** (1 row: contiguous letters; `_cols_per_aa() = 1`). Click→AA uses `aa_col // _cols_per_aa()`. Vertical + horizontal centring pads identical to SynthesisEditor.
* **N-/-C polarity markers.** `_FLANK_MARKER_N = "N-"`, `_FLANK_MARKER_C = "-C"`, width 2. Different from DNA's `5'-`/`-3'` (width 3) — the cursor / click math reads the protein editor's own constant, not the DNA editor's. `_FLANK_MARKER_STYLE = "bold #FF6B9D"` (pink) — visually distinct from DNA's `bold #4CC4FF` (cyan).
* **Codon-table dropdown is protein-tab-only.** Hosted in `#syn-protein-controls` row at the top of the protein TabPane. `Select.Changed` → `_apply_codon_table_choice(choice)` → rebuilds `_codon_table_raw` from `_codon_tables_load()` and calls `pe.set_codon_table(raw)` which rebuilds `_codon_cache: dict[aa, most_freq_codon]`. Default seeded from the first registry entry on `_init_codon_table()` (always K12 per `_codon_tables_load` invariant). Unknown AAs fall back to `NNN`; missing stop falls back to `TAA`.
* **Sacred — `Alt+T` toggles codon mode, NOT codon picking.** `action_toggle_codon_mode` only operates on the protein tab; DNA-tab activation just notifies. Button label flips between "Codon-translated" and "AA-only". Bound to **Alt+T** (NOT Ctrl+M) because Ctrl+M is the CR control character ^M at the terminal protocol level — the terminal swallows it as Enter before Textual sees it. Alt+T sends an ESC-prefixed sequence that's unambiguous across every supported terminal.
* **Built-in protein motif library.** `_PROTEIN_MOTIFS` constant (30 entries) covers affinity tags (His6/8/10, FLAG/3xFLAG, HA, Myc, V5, Strep-II, T7), localisation signals (NLS x2, NES), linkers (GSG, GGS, (GGGGS)x3/x4, EAAAK x3), protease sites (TEV, PreScission, Thrombin, Factor Xa, Furin), self-cleaving 2A peptides (P2A, T2A, E2A, F2A), and motifs (Kozak M, Stop, FLAG+Stop). Same shape as `_load_features()` entries so the side-panel renderer can reuse the row-build code. Insert splices the motif's AA sequence at cursor via `pe.insert_at_cursor`.
* **Atomic save lock (`_save_lock: threading.RLock`).** Both `_commit_save` (DNA) and `_commit_protein_save` wrap their SeqRecord construction + `LibraryPanel.add_entry` call in `with self._save_lock:`. Prevents two rapid Ctrl+S presses (or DNA+protein concurrent save races) from interleaving construction. RLock because the inner library save chain re-acquires `_cache_lock` — those are separate locks, but if either becomes re-entrant the RLock is forward-compatible.
* **Protein save round-trip via CDS `translation=` qualifier.** `_commit_protein_save` back-translates AA → DNA using `_codon_cache` (most-frequent codon per AA), builds linear DNA SeqRecord with CDS feature carrying `translation=[aa_seq]`, `label=[name]`, and `note=` describing the source codon table. `_load_protein_entry_by_id` → `_extract_protein_from_record` reads the CDS's translation qualifier FIRST (preserves AA exactly, no codon-table-drift), falls back to BioPython `Seq.translate()` only when no CDS w/ translation found. Active codon table name embedded in the saved CDS `note=` qualifier for audit trail.
* **`Ctrl+F` / `Ctrl+R` on protein tab notify only.** DNA-specific actions surface a friendly notify pointing at the motif library when invoked on the protein tab. Avoids partial-implementation traps.
* **Logging events:** `synthesis.protein.*` (new / load / save / rename / mode_toggle / codon_table_changed / motif_insert).

## [INV-55] Synthesis composer hardening sweep #14 (2026-05-20)
Post-commit audit of the freshly-shipped synthesis composer. Five fixes, tests in `TestUniqueEntryIdHelper`, `TestSaveAsDoublePromptFix`, `TestPartsBinAutoTriggerNewPart`, `TestSynthesisCloneFragmentButton`, `TestSynthesisCloneFragmentFlow`, `TestSynthesisRenderExceptNarrowed`. Key invariants:
* **Save As prompts ONCE.** Pre-fix `_save_as._on_named` set `_loaded_id=None` then called `_do_save`, which saw the cleared id and pushed a SECOND `NamePlasmidModal` (default = the just-typed name). Post-fix the inner callback builds a unique id via `_make_unique_entry_id` and calls `_commit_save` directly — only one prompt ever fires. Same fix in `_protein_save_as`. Regression-guarded by `test_save_as_commits_without_second_prompt` (counts NamePlasmidModal pushes through a stubbed `app.push_screen`).
* **`_make_unique_entry_id(name, *, exclude_id=None)` is the new entry-id factory for fresh-save / Save-As paths.** Sanitises `name` via `_make_entry_id` then suffix-disambiguates against current `_load_library()` ids (`base`, `base_2`, `base_3`, …, capped at 10_000, then random-hex fall-through). `exclude_id` lets re-save paths pass their current id and skip the bump so the document model (id-match silent replace) keeps working. Mirrors the suffix loop `_domesticator_library_mirror_worker` already used for the same problem. Sacred — pre-fix a synthesis save whose sanitised name collided with an existing library id silently clobbered the unrelated entry via `LibraryPanel.add_entry`'s id-match branch.
* **`PartsBinModal(auto_trigger_new_part=True)`** auto-fires `_new_part(None)` from `on_mount` via `call_after_refresh`. Flag self-clears after firing so a screen-resume can't re-trigger. Sole entry path is `SynthesisScreen.action_clone_fragment` — the Clone Fragment workflow's "open Parts Bin pre-armed for the just-saved fragment" step.
* **Clone Fragment button — Synthesis → Domesticator handoff.** `action_clone_fragment` auto-saves the synthesis fragment (or refuses if empty), re-reads the saved entry via `_find_library_entry_by_id`, loads it onto the main canvas via `app._apply_record(rec)`, dismisses the Synthesis screen, then `call_after_refresh` pushes `PartsBinModal(auto_trigger_new_part=True, clone_prefill_seq=full_seq)`. DNA tab only — protein tab notifies (domestication is a DNA operation). Closes the "compose → domesticate → L0 part in bin" loop in one click; pre-sweep was a 5-click chore.
* **Atomic prefill of Domesticator direct-input.** `DomesticatorModal(prefill_direct_seq=...)` writes the full fragment sequence into the `#dom-direct-seq` TextArea via `ta.text = seq` on mount — one atomic write, so the user can't catch the prefill mid-paste. `PartsBinModal._clone_prefill_seq` is a one-shot attribute consumed by the first `_new_part` call and cleared after firing, so a subsequent manual "New Part" click opens an empty form. Sequence sourced from the just-saved `SeqRecord.seq` (not the editor buffer) to guarantee what lands in the Domesticator matches what landed in the library, byte-for-byte. Logged as `domesticator.prefill { source: "clone_fragment" }`.
* **Single-entry lookups use `_find_library_entry_by_id`.** `_load_entry_by_id` (DNA load) and `_load_protein_entry_by_id` (protein load) swapped their full `for e in _load_library():` walk for the optimised helper added in `[INV-51]`. Removes the per-click O(N) deepcopy on libraries with many entries.
* **Render-path `except Exception` narrowed.** Three guards around `txt.split("\n")` calls in `SynthesisEditor` / `ProteinEditor` render paths (the bottom-strand splice, the horizontal-centring pad, the protein editor's pad) tightened to `(AttributeError, TypeError)` per pitfall #1. `TestSynthesisRenderExceptNarrowed` white-box asserts no bare `except Exception:` remains in either editor's class body so the regression can't drift back.

## [INV-56] Synthesis library entries land as pre-colored features + Edit buttons (sweep #15, 2026-05-20)
Both feature libraries (DNA + Protein) now produce colored features on insert AND let the user edit entries in place. Tests in `TestProteinEditorFeatures`, `TestProteinMotifInsertColored`, `TestProteinSaveLoadRoundTrip`, `TestProteinMotifsPersistence`, `TestSynthesisEditButtons`. Key invariants:
* **`ProteinEditor` grew `_aa_feats: list[dict]`** in AA coords (half-open). `load(aa_seq, feats=...)` seeds the list; `get_feats()` returns a deepcopy; `add_feature(feat)` appends with bounds clamping. Render paints feature cells as background color via per-cell `feat_bg` lookup; priority is `cursor (reverse) > selection (#003366) > feature color > default`, so the cursor + selection always stay visually loud over the colored band. Both render modes (codon-translated + AA-only) implement the same overlay.
* **Insert / delete shift AA features.** `insert_at_cursor` shifts features whose `start >= cur` right by the insert length; `end >= cur` extends UNLESS the feature is a zero-length anchor parked at the cursor. `_delete_range` clips overlapping features to the surviving span, shifts entirely-right features left by the delete length, drops zero-survival features. Mirrors `SynthesisEditor` feature shift semantics so behaviour matches across tabs.
* **Motif insert produces a colored feature.** `_motif_insert_selected` captures `pe._cursor_pos` BEFORE the splice (so it has the start of the inserted span), calls `pe.insert_at_cursor(aa_seq)`, then `pe.add_feature(...)` over `[start, start+len(aa_seq))`. Color sourced from the motif's own `color` field when set (user edits), falling back to `_PROTEIN_FEATURE_TYPE_COLORS[feature_type]` — a 6-entry palette for `Tag` / `Signal` / `Linker` / `Cleavage` / `2A peptide` / `Motif`.
* **Save/load round-trip via CDS sub-features.** `_commit_protein_save` writes each AA feature as a `misc_feature` SeqFeature inside the CDS (AA coords × 3 → DNA coords), stashing the AA feature type in `qualifiers["note"]` as `"splicecraft-aa-feature-type=<type>"` so the GenBank vocabulary mismatch (Tag/Signal/Linker aren't standard) round-trips. Color is also written via `ApEinfo_fwdcolor` / `ApEinfo_revcolor` so external viewers (ApE / CommercialSaaS) see the band. `_extract_aa_feats_from_record` reads the note marker back and recovers the original `type` string; falls back to `"Motif"` if missing.
* **`protein_motifs.json` persistence + Edit button.** New `_PROTEIN_MOTIFS_FILE = _DATA_DIR / "protein_motifs.json"` + cache. `_load_protein_motifs()` merges built-in `_PROTEIN_MOTIFS` with user overrides keyed on `name` (user edit replaces the built-in by name; novel user motifs append to the end). `_save_protein_motifs(entries)` writes ONLY user-modified entries; the merge happens on read. Synthesis motif Edit button → `AddFeatureModal(prefill=motif)` → on save, upserts to user file by name (copy-on-write — built-ins stay in code as a fallback). Synthesis feature-library Edit button (DNA tab) does the same against `features.json` — same `(name, feature_type)` key + dedup-on-rename logic `FeatureLibraryScreen.action_edit` uses, so edits made here surface in the standalone Features screen immediately. Conftest fixture seeds `_PROTEIN_MOTIFS_FILE` into the isolated path; `_check_data_files` validates the file at launch.

## [INV-57] Sweep #16 — dithered protein lane art + distinct motif colors + CI lint gate (2026-05-21)
Two visual polish items + one infra fix. Tests in `TestProteinMotifsDistinctColors`, `TestProteinDitherRender`, `TestProteinMotifInsertColorIsUnique`. Key invariants:
* **Each built-in motif carries its own `color`.** Pre-sweep all 10 Tag motifs shared `#3B82F6`, all 5 Linkers shared grey, etc. — the user couldn't tell two His tags apart in the side panel. Now `_PROTEIN_MOTIFS` carries a curated 30-entry palette spread within each family (Tags shift blue→cyan→indigo→violet; Cleavage reds→pinks; 2A peptides through the greens). Motif insert reads `motif.get("color")` first so the user's per-motif distinct color always wins over the family-fallback. User edits via the Edit button persist to `protein_motifs.json` and override these defaults.
* **Palette-key bug fix: `"2A peptide"` → `"2A"`.** `_PROTEIN_FEATURE_TYPE_COLORS` carried `"2A peptide"` as a key while `_PROTEIN_MOTIFS` used `"2A"` for the `feature_type` — silent fallback to `Motif` purple for every 2A insert. Fixed by matching the key to the data; `TestProteinMotifsDistinctColors.test_palette_key_matches_motif_feature_types` regression-guards.
* **ProteinEditor multi-lane dithered feature art (`_build_protein_lane_text`).** Mirrors the main seq panel's `_chunk_lane_groups` + `_paint_feature_bar` + `_paint_feature_label` style. AA-coord 2D greedy pack via `_pack_features_2d`-equivalent inline loop. Each feature occupies 2 rows: row 0 = `▒`-block bar adjacent to AA (with `▶`/`◀` strand-arrowhead glyph at the terminus), row 1 = centred label in `bold {color}`. Overlapping motifs stack into separate lanes; non-overlapping pack into the same lane. Multi-lane art is prepended ONLY when ≥1 feature is present — the empty case keeps the original 2-row (codon mode) / 1-row (AA-only) layout so users who haven't inserted a motif see the same view they always did. Replaces the per-cell background-color paint from sweep #15 (which was visually busy on overlapping motifs and didn't match the seq panel). `_row_count` tracks the actual lane height via `_lane_height()` so vertical centring stays correct. Legacy `_build_dither_row(cols_per_aa=...)` retained as a single-line back-compat alias that returns just the bar row (closest to AA) — used by simple consumers and white-box tests.
* **`release.py` runs `ruff check .` BEFORE pytest.** Pre-sweep, lint failures only surfaced in CI; a local release passed pytest + built wheels + pushed → CI badge went red on the lint job afterward. Now `release.py` runs the same `ruff check .` invocation `.github/workflows/test.yml`'s `lint` job uses — a release that lints clean here also lints clean in CI. Pyright deliberately NOT added (slow + flaky offline JS-pinned binary; stays CI-only via `PYRIGHT_PYTHON_FORCE_VERSION=latest`).
* **`test_delete_*` smoke tests use poll helper.** `TestDeleteFocusRouting._await_row_count(app, target, pilot)` polls the library DataTable for the expected `row_count` with up to 20 × 50 ms ticks, breaking early on convergence. Pre-sweep all four delete tests used a single fixed `await pilot.pause(0.05)` after `modal.dismiss(True)`, which on CI's Python 3.12 runner intermittently observed the row count one tick before the DataTable repopulate landed → red badge. Common case still completes in <100 ms.

## [INV-58] Sweep #17 — insert-at-cursor boundary-extend kwarg (2026-05-21)
`SynthesisEditor.insert_at_cursor` + `ProteinEditor.insert_at_cursor` gained `extend_adjacent_feats: bool = True` kwarg. Tests in `TestProteinMotifNonOverlapAtBoundary`, `TestDnaFeatLibNonOverlapAtBoundary`. Key invariants:
* **Strictly-inside extension is sacred.** A feature spanning `[fs, fe)` with cursor `fs < cur < fe` ALWAYS extends on insert regardless of the kwarg — typing inside a feature's body grows the bar, matching the seq-panel feel. Both kwarg branches preserve this: the only difference is the BOUNDARY case (`cur == fe`).
* **Default `extend_adjacent_feats=True` keeps the legacy typing-at-tail behaviour.** A feature whose `end == cursor` extends to swallow the inserted bases — the "extend the feature I'm appending to" semantics. Used by the user-typing path (`on_key` → `insert_at_cursor(ch)`) and by Ctrl+R restriction-site inserts (the user is adding bases into existing context).
* **`extend_adjacent_feats=False` lands separate library entries cleanly.** `_motif_insert_selected` (protein) and `_featlib_insert_selected` (DNA) pass False so that when the user inserts a second feature at the 3' boundary of an existing one (`cur == prev.end`), the prior feature's `end` stays put and the new feature lands as a sibling in `[cur, cur+n_ins)`. Pre-fix the upstream feature swelled to cover the insert AND the new feature stacked on top — visible overlap in the dithered lane art, two lanes where there should be one.
* **5' boundary (cursor at fs) shifts the existing feature right.** Both branches always shift `fs >= cur` regardless of the kwarg — the insert sits BEFORE the feature's first AA/bp by definition. Verified by `test_motif_insert_at_5p_boundary_shifts_existing`.
* **Delete-from-inside contracts the bar.** `_delete_range` clips overlap to the surviving span; entirely-right features shift left by the delete length; zero-survival features drop. Unchanged by sweep #17 — listed here only because the user's bug report bundled "extend / contract" as paired invariants.

## [INV-59] Sweep #18 — protein-load filter tightening + CI trimmed (2026-05-21)
Audit follow-up plus CI cycle-speed improvement. `SynthesisScreen._extract_aa_feats_from_record` had two sub-issues: the type filter was too loose (`if sf.type == "CDS": continue` — accepted gene/RBS/mat_peptide/etc. inside the CDS) and codon-misaligned coords would silently floor to the wrong AA boundaries. Fixed by:
* **`if sf.type != "misc_feature": continue`** as the type filter. Strict match to what `_commit_protein_save` writes — keeps imported GenBank records with unrelated CDS sub-features from polluting the Protein-tab editor.
* **Codon-alignment guard.** Drop any sub-feature whose `(fs - cds_s) % 3 != 0` or `(fe - cds_s) % 3 != 0`. Save-path always emits `AA × 3` aligned coords, so well-formed sub-features pass; mis-aligned coords (from a hand-edited GenBank import) get dropped instead of mapped to the wrong residue boundaries.
* Tests in `TestProteinLoadFilterStrict` (8 cases) cover misc_feature kept, gene/RBS/mat_peptide dropped, mis-aligned dropped, mixed-batch only-misc-aligned-passes, and the existing round-trip still works.
* **CI trimmed for fast iteration.** Pre-sweep `.github/workflows/test.yml` ran a 4-Python matrix (3.10/3.11/3.12/3.13) + a separate coverage job — ~28 min wall-clock per push. Trimmed to lint + single-Python-3.12 pytest with `-n auto` xdist parallelism on 2-core GitHub runners — ~6-8 min wall-clock. `pyproject.toml` still declares `requires-python = ">=3.10"`; multi-version verification stays the responsibility of `release.py` running locally pre-tag. CI's job is the public canary on master, not the matrix gate.
* **release.py bundles accumulated work.** Pre-sweep `_ensure_clean_tree` refused to proceed with any uncommitted changes — every feature paid a `git commit` toll. Sweep #18: the new `_summarize_pending_changes` prints `git status --short` (informational only); commit step uses `git add -A` to bundle all modified + new tracked files into the single `Release vX.Y.Z` commit. `.gitignore` still gates artefacts. Frees the iteration cycle — work accumulates in the tree until release.

## [INV-60] Sweep #19 — synthesis perf + pyright (2026-05-21)
Two fixes:
* **Protein lane art was O(rows × cells) on `Text.append`.** `ProteinEditor._build_protein_lane_text` allocated a `total_rows × total_cells` grid and called `Text.append` per cell — 354 ms at the 16.6k-AA cap with 30 motifs, 2.5 s in the all-overlap worst case. Replaced with **per-row segment lists**: each feature contributes O(1) `(col_s, col_e, text, style)` segments to its bar + label rows; emission walks each row's segments sorted-by-col_s and `Text.append`s ONE segment per feature plus inter-feature blank padding. Result: ≥10× speedup across realistic scenarios (5k AA × 30 motifs: 84 ms → 5 ms), 31× on the all-overlap worst case (2.5 s → 81 ms). `scripts/perf_probe_synthesis.py` captures the bench; rerun on any future lane-art change.
* **`self.app._apply_record` pyright fix.** `App[Unknown]` (Textual's base type) doesn't declare `_apply_record` — that's PlasmidApp-only. Added `# type: ignore[attr-defined]` at the synthesis Clone-Fragment call site. Same `# type: ignore` pattern other PlasmidApp-only attribute accesses use; pyright's `[tool.pyright]` analysis is limited to `splicecraft.py` + sidecars so this is the only place the warning surfaced in CI's lint job.

## [INV-61] Sweep #20 — agent-API gaps + event logger + invariant retrofit (2026-05-21)
Multi-part audit found drift accumulating in the wake of sweeps #15–#19. `tests/test_sweep20.py` (70 cases). Highlights:
* **21 new agent endpoints across four data systems + codon-table preference that had ZERO side-door access.** Pre-sweep, experiments / experiment_projects / gels / protein_motifs all shipped with full `_load_*` / `_save_*` infrastructure but external agents couldn't list / create / update / delete any of them. Added: `list-experiments` / `get-experiment` / `create-experiment` / `update-experiment` / `delete-experiment`; `list-experiment-projects` / `set-active-experiment-project` / `create-experiment-project` / `rename-experiment-project` / `delete-experiment-project`; `list-gels` / `get-gel` / `create-gel` / `update-gel` / `delete-gel`; `list-protein-motifs` / `set-protein-motif` / `delete-protein-motif`; `auto-detect-entry-vectors` / `clear-entry-vectors-for-grammar`; `set-active-codon-table` (with new `active_codon_table` settings key honored by `SynthesisScreen._init_codon_table`; existing `list-codon-tables` extended with `active_taxid` field). Total handlers 64 → 85. Project-switch endpoint mirrors the GUI's `[INV-43]` atomicity pattern (active-pointer write + `_settings_flush_sync()` + experiments.json write). Protein-motif endpoints round-trip through the user-file ↔ built-in merge in `_load_protein_motifs`; deleting a user override restores the built-in, deleting a built-in directly is rejected 404.
* **`_MASTER_DELETE_CACHE_ATTRS` (`[INV-48]`) missing two caches.** `_primer_usage_cache` (line 27694, added in primer-design refactor) and `_feature_library_index_cache` (line 28264, added in parts-bin perf work) had never been registered. Master Delete would wipe the files but leave the in-memory references alive; a fresh `_load_*` call would have rebuilt them, but a stale cache hit between the wipe and the next read could surface ghost data. Now covered. `TestMasterDeleteCacheAttrsCoverage.test_no_persisted_data_cache_missing` mirrors the conftest data-file list so a future feature can't silently add a `*_cache` global without registering it.
* **`_blocks_undo: bool = True` (`[INV-41]`) missing on five input-bearing modals.** Sweep #12 added the flag to 12 modals; five had been overlooked: `FetchModal` (URL + email Inputs), `OpenFileModal` (filename Input), `ExportGenBankModal` (filename Input), `NcbiTaxonPickerModal` (search-query Input), `FeatureSearchModal` (search-query Input). Pre-fix, a Ctrl+Z inside any of these typing-heavy modals fell through to the canvas record's undo stack instead of the Input's own undo, surprising the user mid-edit. Attr placement AFTER docstring per the invariant.
* **Collision modals (`ExactCopyConfirmModal`, `NameCollisionModal`) dismissed silently.** No structured event for the user's Skip / Keep / Overwrite / Cancel choice — `[INV-42]`'s "user pastes log → AI parses → patch" loop couldn't reconstruct the decision. Now every exit path emits `collision.exact_copy.dismiss` or `collision.name_collision.dismiss` with `{entity, n, choice}` fields where `choice ∈ {skip, keep, overwrite, cancel, esc}`.
* **`_save_protein_motifs` GUI failure path routed through `_notify_save_failure`.** Pre-sweep the GUI motif-edit save did its own `_log.exception` + inline notify, bypassing the `save.failed` structured event the rest of the codebase emits via `_notify_save_failure`. Now consistent with every other GUI save path.
* **`@_action_log` retrofit on 15 must-have actions.** Per `[INV-42]` ("`@_action_log(event_name)` decorates every `action_*`"), an Explore-audit pass found 25 user-visible action methods lacking the decorator. Sweep #20 added to 15 of those — the ones where no body event already captures the action (avoid double-logging): `FeatureLibraryScreen` (export_fasta / close / save / add / edit / rename / remove); `EntryVectorsModal.action_close`; `PartsBinModal.action_delete_selected_parts`; `ExperimentsScreen.action_open_projects`; `ExperimentRenameModal.action_submit`; `AlignmentScreen.action_close`; `HistoryScreen.action_close`; `FeatureSidebar.action_open_feature_at_cursor`; `SequencePanel.action_open_selected_feature`. The 10 SKIPPED actions already emit body events (`synthesis.*` / `experiments.new` / `experiments.save` / `undo.trigger` / `redo.trigger`) or are widget-level delegates whose App-level wrapper already carries the decorator (`PlasmidMap.action_toggle_map_view` is called by `PlasmidApp.action_toggle_map_view` which has `app.toggle.map_view`).
* **Bare-`except` narrowing pass.** 4 of the 286 `except Exception:` clauses outside `@work` bodies tightened to narrow types: 2 in `_build_system_info`/`_create_diagnostic_bundle` (file-system enumeration + JSON serialisation), 2 in the inner `_load_settings` and `_read_log_tail` calls inside `_collect_ui_snapshot` (internal helpers don't go through user-provided `__getattr__`). **The 6 getattr blocks in `_collect_ui_snapshot` that touch the `app` argument STAY broad** with `# noqa: BLE001 — broken-app survival` comments — `tests/test_smoke.py::test_collect_ui_snapshot_handles_broken_app` constructs a `__getattr__`-raises-RuntimeError stub and asserts the snapshot survives. **Sacred lesson**: diagnostic-time helpers that inspect potentially-broken state need broad catches by design. The first narrowing attempt failed the test; reverted with comments. The remaining ~278 `except Exception:` sites split into three legitimate categories: (a) inside `@work(thread=True)` decorated worker bodies — pitfall #1's explicit carve-out; (b) logging helpers (`_repr_for_log`, `_action_log` wrapper, `_notify_save_failure`) marked `# noqa: BLE001 — logging must never raise`; (c) bridging third-party libraries with loose error models (`primer3.calc_tm`, BioPython aligner) where the broad catch immediately re-raises as a clean `ValueError`. These are all correct and were not touched.
* **Documentation freshness.** README + `docs/agent-api.md` + `docs/features.md` updated from "60+ endpoints" to "80+ endpoints" with the new categories enumerated (experiments / projects / gels / protein motifs / entry vectors).

## [INV-62] Sweep #21 — modal-staleness audit (2026-05-21)
Audit pass for the staleness window: a modal opens at T=0 capturing canvas state (feature idx, selection range, record reference); between T=0 and dismiss at T=2, an agent-API endpoint (`_h_load_entry` / `_h_fetch` / `_h_load_file`) can mutate `_current_record`; the modal's dismiss callback then applies user edits to the WRONG molecule at the WRONG idx. `tests/test_sweep21.py` (7 cases). Key fixes:
* **`_open_feature_editor` callback wrapped via `_guard_callback`.** Pre-sweep, FeatureEditModal / PrimerEditModal captured `idx` at T=0 and the dismiss callback applied the edit unconditionally. An agent-driven `_h_load_entry` mid-modal swapped `_current_record` and the user's save landed on the new record's feature[idx] — silent data corruption on the wrong molecule. Now uses the same `_guard_callback` wrapper that `action_edit_seq` already used.
* **`action_add_feature` callback wrapped via `_guard_callback`.** Same risk on the "Annotate" path — AddFeatureModal captured selection `(start, end)` at T=0; applying those coords to a NEW record's bases would silently corrupt the annotation. Wrap-aware location math doesn't help when the underlying bases are entirely different DNA.
* **`_guard_callback` is the canonical helper.** Lives at `PlasmidApp._guard_callback`; captures `_record_load_counter` at wrap time, refuses to fire the wrapped callback if the counter shifted (every `_apply_record` bumps it). Pre-sweep, only `action_edit_seq` used it; sweep #21 brings `_open_feature_editor` + `action_add_feature` to parity. `action_transfer_annotations` uses an inline counter check (lines 73066-73082) because its state spans two modal callbacks; same semantic.
* **What was checked but NOT fixed (verified-safe):** PartEditModal already does identity-based lookup `(name, sequence)` on save and refuses with a friendly notify if the part identity has been removed/modified by another writer — better than counter-based since it handles in-place mutations too. GrammarEditorModal serializes through `_cache_lock` via `_save_custom_grammars`. Both safe.

## [INV-63] Sweep #22 — load-bearing function audit + file-migration futureproofing (2026-05-21)
Four parallel deep audits (I/O atomicity, sequence primitives, concurrency/state, logging plumbing) covering 17 functions across the codebase's most-used helpers. Most verified clean; two real defects fixed, one minor polish. Plus a futureproofing pass to make sure files imported from previous SpliceCraft versions migrate correctly. `tests/test_sweep22.py` (20 cases).
* **`_safe_save_json` ancestor-symlink walk (HIGH).** Pre-sweep, only `path.is_symlink()` was checked. Sweep #10 (`[INV-50]`) added the full ancestor-chain walk to `_check_agent_write_path` but didn't propagate to `_safe_save_json` — a symlink at ANY deeper ancestor (e.g. `~/.local` → `/etc`) could redirect every save under the data dir. Now mirrors the agent-side defense: walks every ancestor up to the filesystem root, refuses on any symlink hit, surfaces a friendly OSError naming the symlinked ancestor. Errors mid-walk also refuse — can't tell if it's safe.
* **`_iupac_pattern` case-fold cache key (LOW polish).** Cache keys preserved input case (`"GAATTC"` vs `"gaattc"` occupied separate slots) but the internal regex was always built from `site.upper()`. Two slots, identical regex. Now normalizes the key to uppercase on lookup AND store. Saves cache slots on mixed-case calls; one-line fix in `splicecraft_biology.py`.
* **File migration framework — test coverage (futureproofing).** `_CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` + `_ENTRY_MIGRATIONS` + `_migrate_entries` + `_extract_entries` (`[INV-36]`) have been in place since ~0.5.x but NEVER exercised in production because no migrations are registered. When the schema finally bumps from v1 to v2, the framework needs to work correctly. `TestMigrationFramework` (11 cases) covers every path: bare-list legacy → v0 migrated; envelope v1 → no-op; envelope future-version → loads with warning; multi-step chain (v0→v1→v2); missing intermediate migrator → no-op pass-through; failed migrator → entry preserved + warning; non-dict garbage → skipped; non-list input → empty list + warning; no-op when at current → fresh list returned. `TestLegacyFileRoundTrip` verifies a bare-list file loaded + re-saved comes back as the envelope format. `TestMigrationCoverageEveryLoadPath` white-box asserts every `_load_*` helper routes through `_safe_load_json` so the framework applies uniformly — a future load helper that bypasses it would silently skip migrations.
* **`.dna` packet XML size-cap (declined-by-design).** The Explore agent flagged that `_iter_commercialsaas_packets` payloads pass to `_safe_xml_parse` without per-packet caps. Verified: the upstream `load_genbank` size check at `_SAFE_LOAD_JSON_MAX_BYTES = 1 GB` bounds the entire `.dna` file. Billion-laughs requires a DTD which `_safe_xml_parse` rejects. The remaining risk (legit-but-large XML packet → memory pressure) is "user opens their own 1 GB file" — not a remote-attacker DoS. Declined as defense-in-depth not worth the complexity.
* **Audit category 1 — I/O atomicity verified clean otherwise:** backup ordering correct (write `.bak` + `.bak.<ts>` BEFORE new content lands), atomic write sequence intact (mkstemp → fsync → close → os.replace → fsync_parent_dir), no quadratic encode patterns in JSON serialise, `_safe_file_size_check` correctly rejects symlinks/FIFOs/devices via lstat, DOCTYPE/ENTITY defense in `_safe_xml_parse` is case-insensitive streaming prologue scan (handles whitespace + comment prefixes).
* **Audit category 2 — sequence primitives verified clean:** all sacred invariants #1–#9 hold. `_rc` IUPAC complement covers all 15 codes via stdlib `str.translate`; `_scan_restriction_sites` palindromic-forward-only + wrap-boundary + recut-modulo math all correct; `_feat_bounds` handles canonical CompoundLocation wrap re-encoding (start=0, end=total signal); `_smallest_enclosing_feature` bisect on `_feats_starts_sorted` correct + wrap-feature fallback bounded.
* **Audit category 3 — concurrency verified clean:** `_apply_record` orders `_current_record =` BEFORE `_record_load_counter += 1` so a worker reading the counter sees the new record (not torn state); every full-record-swap site bumps the counter; `_cache_lock` (RLock) wraps save+mirror in a single critical section across all 13 `_save_*` helpers per sweep #10 `[INV-50]`; `_LIVE_APP_REF` lifecycle clean (set in `on_mount` + `_agent_dispatch`, cleared in `finally`).
* **Audit category 4 — logging plumbing verified clean:** `_repr_for_log` catches `SeqRecord` / `Seq` / `MutableSeq` / `bytes` / `bytearray` via class-name match (no BioPython import); `_log_event` 200-char string truncation + 300-char nested-dict bail; `_action_log` and `_timed` both use `@functools.wraps` + `_stacklevel=3` correctly; `_log.propagate = False` blocks BioPython's child-logger output from inheriting our level. Sacred `[INV-38]` (sequence content never leaked) verified — no `_log_event` callsite passes raw sequence fields.

## [INV-64] Sweep #23 — file-format expansion + agent CRUD parity + deferred-fix landing (2026-05-21)
Multi-week sweep covering format support, agent-API CRUD gaps, modal hardening, and several perf optimizations. New tests in `tests/test_new_formats.py` (17 cases) + `tests/test_new_agent_endpoints.py` (13 cases) + 1 new case in `tests/test_sweep10.py`. Total handler count 89 → 107.

* **Five new file formats accepted by the loader chain.** EMBL (`.embl`), AB1 Sanger trace (`.ab1`/`.abi`), FASTQ multi-read (`.fastq`/`.fq`, capped at `_FASTQ_MAX_READS = 1000` reads per file), GFF3 standalone (`.gff`/`.gff3` with inline `##FASTA`), and GFF3 overlay (sequence-less → applied to currently-loaded record). Dispatch is uniform across the three import paths: UI `OpenFileModal._do_load`, `_bulk_import_folder`, and agent `_h_load_file` all consult `_is_fasta_path` / `_is_ab1_path` / `_is_fastq_path` / `.gff[3]` suffix in the same order before falling through to `load_genbank`. `_detect_plasmid_format` now content-sniffs unknown extensions (LOCUS / ID / CommercialSaaS cookie peek) before defaulting to GenBank. **`_check_export_extension` extended to the three text-format UI export modals** — pre-fix the agent path enforced extension allow-lists but the UI path silently wrote GenBank text to a `.dna` filename.

* **`GFF3 standalone-vs-overlay split` is the canonical pattern.** A GFF3 file with an inline `##FASTA` section is a complete record → `_gff3_path_to_record` builds a fresh `SeqRecord` and the UI `load-file` flow replaces the canvas. A sequence-less GFF3 is a feature overlay → `_gff3_apply_to_loaded_record` mutates the loaded record's `.features` in place after length-mismatch validation. The two paths refuse each other's input (`_gff3_apply_to_loaded_record` rejects `##FASTA` payloads to prevent silent sequence loss; `_gff3_path_to_record` rejects sequence-less files to prevent feature drift onto the wrong canvas). **Wrap features round-trip via same-`ID=` row rejoining** (inverse of pitfall #26's `_record_to_gff3` split-row emit). Agent endpoint `apply-gff3` mirrors `transfer-annotations`'s record-load-counter stale-canvas guard.

* **Bulk export collection feature.** New `_bulk_export_collection(collection_name, fmt, target_dir, progress_cb)` writes every plasmid in a collection to a folder as individual files. GenBank format uses a fast path that atomic-writes the cached `gb_text` directly without a Biopython round-trip (skips ~2–10 s on 500-plasmid exports). Other formats parse-and-emit via the existing per-format writers. **Filename collision policy: case-folded uniqueness set + `_N` suffix bump.** Pre-fix the exact-case check missed `pUC19` / `puc19` collisions on APFS/NTFS → silent overwrite. The case-fold defense mirrors `_dna_sidecar_path`. `_safe_export_filename` scrubs path-traversal characters + Windows reserved device names via the hoisted `_WIN_RESERVED_FILENAMES` + `_is_windows_reserved_stem` helpers (three inline duplicates collapsed to one). UI: `BulkExportCollectionModal` with collection / format / folder pickers + worker-driven progress; menu entry `File → Export collection (bulk)…`. Agent endpoint `bulk-export-collection`.

* **18 new agent endpoints across four data systems** brought to full CRUD parity:
  * **Cloning grammars:** `list-grammars`, `get-grammar`, `create-grammar`, `update-grammar`, `delete-grammar`. Built-in ids (`gb_l0`, `moclo_plant`) refused for create/update/delete — mirrors the UI's `editable=False` gate. Delete also clears the bound entry vector for the grammar.
  * **Primers:** `list-primers`, `get-primer`, `create-primer`, `delete-primer` (update was already present). New `list-primer-collections` + `set-active-primer-collection` for collection-aware workflows. Duplicate sequences (case-insensitive) refused with 409 + the existing entry's name returned for disambiguation.
  * **Parts bin:** `create-part`, `update-part` (list/get/delete were already present). Lookup is `(name, grammar)`; update preserves original date so an edit doesn't masquerade as a fresh add.
  * **Feature library:** `list-feature-library`, `get-feature-library`, `create-feature-library`, `update-feature-library`, `delete-feature-library`. Named distinctly from the record-level `add/delete/update/get-feature` (which operate on the loaded plasmid's features) to prevent agent-side confusion — the record-level handlers were defined first and the name collision was caught by pyright at landing time.

* **`_AGENT_BACKUP_LABELS` parity invariant.** Pre-sweep `_AGENT_BACKUP_LABELS` covered 11 of 16 `_USER_DATA_FILE_ATTRS` and `RestoreFromBackupModal._TARGETS` covered 9 of 16. Users could list/restore Experiments / Gels / Protein motifs / Primer collections via the GUI but not via the agent API; Feature library / Feature colours / Cloning grammars / Entry vectors / Codon tables / Parts-bin collections were unreachable from EITHER path even though their `.bak` files existed on disk. Both maps now mirror `_USER_DATA_FILE_ATTRS` minus `_SETTINGS_FILE` (settings restore is intentionally GUI-only to avoid clobbering live in-session pointer state). `tests/test_sweep10.py::test_agent_backup_labels_parity_with_user_data_files` is the regression guard: asserts every user-data attr except settings is reachable by SOME label AND every label has a cache-bust map entry. **Sacred contract:** any new persisted file added to `_USER_DATA_FILE_ATTRS` must also land in `_AGENT_BACKUP_LABELS` + `RestoreFromBackupModal._TARGETS` + the cache-bust map at `_h_restore_backup:cache_attr`, OR be explicitly excluded with a code comment naming the reason.

* **`_iter_collections_readonly` no-mutation contract.** Callers MUST NOT mutate the returned cache list or its nested dicts. Returns the live cache directly (no `_typed_clone`) for hot read paths — saves ~30–50 ms per call on 5k-plasmid libraries (`_search_collections_library` per-keystroke search, `_blast_collections_fingerprint` on every BLAST submit). For ANY mutation path, use `_load_collections()` (which clones). Mirroring pitfall #17's existing read-side clone rule, but inverted: this helper is the explicit "I won't mutate" handshake. Confirmed safe at all four current callsites by inspection; widening to other typed loaders deferred until a specific caller demonstrates a perf need.

* **BLAST collections fingerprint cache.** `_blast_collections_fingerprint` is memoised by `(id(_collections_cache), frozenset(names))`. Saves a full library walk per `_blast_get_db` call. Auto-invalidates when `_save_collections` reseats the cache (identity flips inside the `_cache_lock` block). Combined with the earlier sweep's content-fingerprint BLAST cache (which dropped the `_blast_clear_cache()` unconditional-clear from `_save_collections`), a 200-plasmid collection rename no longer flushes BLAST dbs — the fingerprint is the same, cache stays warm.

* **`_gb_text_to_record` LRU cache.** 64-entry cache keyed on `hash(text)`. Used by 53 callsites; library-iteration paths (`_classify_part_from_plasmid`, `_assembly_fragment_recover_gb_text`, `_bulk_export_collection` non-GenBank path) repeatedly parse the same `gb_text` during one user action. Cache returns `deepcopy` on hit so callers can mutate freely per pitfall #17.

* **`_h_apply_gff3` apply-record fix (P0 bug).** Pre-fix the handler called nonexistent `app._refresh_canvas_after_edit()` swallowed by an `(AttributeError, Exception)` antipattern — feature counts were returned but the map never repainted. Now follows the canonical `transfer-annotations` pattern: deepcopy `rec` on the worker thread, mutate the copy, hop to UI thread for `_push_undo` + `_apply_record(clear_undo=False)` + `_mark_dirty()`. Record-load-counter captured at entry and re-checked in `_apply` so a concurrent canvas swap surfaces 409 instead of silently corrupting state.

* **`(AttributeError, Exception)` antipattern is bare-except in disguise.** `Exception` already subsumes `AttributeError`, so the tuple is equivalent to `except Exception` — violates pitfall #1. Fixed in `_h_apply_gff3` (two sites) and Sanger handlers (`_sanger_load`, `_sanger_add_to_library`). **Sacred lesson:** when narrowing exception classes, write the tuple AS the union you actually want — pyright won't catch the redundancy and a copy-paste from a worker-thread `except Exception as exc` carve-out propagates as silently-broad.

* **Format-helper size caps.** `_ab1_path_to_record` + `_fastq_path_to_records` + `_gff3_path_to_record` route through `_safe_file_size_check` at entry. Pre-fix `SeqIO.parse(path, "abi")` streamed from disk with no upstream guard — a multi-GB hostile file OOM'd the worker before any packet validation. FASTQ has a second cap (`_FASTQ_MAX_READS = 1000`) — at the 50 MB file cap a typical Illumina lane delivers ≫1000 short reads; the plasmid library wasn't designed to hold raw sequencing reads.

* **`_check_data_files` is validate-only — a seed-from-validate optimisation FAILED.** First attempt at a 2026-05-21 perf win: pre-sweep `_check_data_files` validates every persisted JSON via `_safe_load_json` to surface corruption early; the subsequent first-use `_load_*` call re-reads the same file. The naive optimisation seeded each typed cache with the validated entries to skip the second read. **It broke three loaders whose post-validation logic the generic seed bypassed:** `_load_protein_motifs` merges built-in motifs with user overrides (30 built-ins disappeared), `_load_experiments` runs `_migrate_legacy_tag_format` (pre-2026-05-18 tag tokens leaked into the editor), `_load_entry_vectors` applies a tighter filter requiring `grammar_id` to be a string (drift entries leaked). **Reverted to validate-only.** Future-me: do not re-attempt without isolating each loader's post-load logic into a shared helper that runs on both seed and lazy-load paths. Documented here so the audit recommendation doesn't re-surface as actionable.

* **`_dismiss_once` retrofit scoped to mutating modals.** Sweep #21 found ~75 `ModalScreen` subclasses lacking the `[INV-50]` double-fire guard. Indiscriminately retrofitting all 75 would have added maintenance noise to view-only pickers that have no destructive payload. Scope landed: every modal that **writes to disk on dismiss OR dismisses with a payload that triggers a downstream save** — 13 destructive/quit/export/save-prompt modals (UnsavedQuitModal, QuitConfirmModal, UnsavedNavigateModal, EditGrammarConfirmModal, `_ConfirmDeleteGrammarModal`, LargeFileConfirmModal, ExperimentDeleteConfirmModal, ExperimentUnsavedChangesModal, SynthesisUnsavedChangesModal, ExportGenBankModal, ExportCommercialSaaSModal, GffExportModal, FastaExportModal). View-only pickers (PlasmidPickerModal, NamePickerModal, etc.) left unguarded — they round-trip a selection without mutating state, so a double-dismiss races to the same payload twice with no consequence beyond a redundant `dismiss()` exception that Textual already swallows. The pattern applied is uniform: `self._dismissed: bool = False` in `__init__`, `_dismiss_once(payload)` helper, every exit path (button + Esc + worker-callback) routes through it. **Sacred contract:** any NEW modal that writes on dismiss must add the guard at landing time, not as a follow-up sweep.

* **`_atomic_write_text` + `_atomic_write_bytes` symlink refusal.** `os.replace(tmp, str(path))` on a symlink replaces the *symlink itself* with a regular file — breaks the link chain without writing through to the target. Pre-fix a user opening `~/work/current.gb` (a symlink into `~/projects/foo/v3.gb`) and saving would silently break the symlink. Now both atomic-write helpers `lstat` the target and raise OSError if `S_ISLNK`. NOT a write-into-target vuln (replace doesn't follow symlinks on rename), but real silent breakage. Users who legitimately want to overwrite through a symlink must resolve the path themselves first.

* **`_backup_info` damaged-row schema.** Returns `{n_entries, mtime_str, error}` for every backup — `n_entries: None` and a non-empty `error` string for parse-failed / oversized / non-list-or-envelope-shaped files. Pre-fix, damaged backups returned `None` and were silently dropped from the Restore UI's row list. A user trying to recover from a corrupted-but-existing snapshot would see "no recoverable backups" and have no way to know the file was there. Now the row surfaces with `[red]<kind> [damaged][/red]` in the kind column and `?` in the entries column; the restore button refuses with a clear "Backup is damaged: {error}" message.

* **Sanger AB1 tab in `SequencingScreen`.** New "Sanger (.ab1)" top-level tab alongside Plasmidsaurus. `_AbiAwareDirectoryTree` paints `.ab1`/`.abi` files sky-blue. Pick a trace → preview (name, length, mean Phred, first 200 bases) → "Load into canvas" or "Add to library" buttons (both gated until a valid trace is parsed). Re-clicking the same path short-circuits — `_ab1_path_to_record` is ~50–200 ms per trace.

* **`docs/features.md` rewrite.** Pre-sweep the file stopped at the original feature surface; missing Synthesis (DNA + Protein composers), Experiments lab notebook, Gels, Protein motifs, Master Delete, the full File-formats matrix. Now mirrors the README's feature taxonomy + adds a dedicated "Recovery + data safety" section covering the four-layer JSON safety net, Restore-from-backup damaged-row tagging, Master Delete, and pre-update snapshots. README endpoint count bumped to "90+"; `docs/agent-api.md` body updated.

* **Performance hot-path fixes.** `_label_at` row-indexed lookup (`splicecraft.py:12509`) — per-hover linear scan replaced with `{ly: [(x0, x1, idx), ...]}` cache invalidated by `id(self._label_bboxes)`. `_update_hover_status` widget-ref cache (`splicecraft.py:17574`) — DOM walk at 60 fps hover collapsed to a one-time `query_one` cached on the panel instance.

* **Settings schema bumps.** `crash_recovery_seen` added to `_SETTINGS_SCHEMA` with `((list,), [])` so a corrupted settings.json with `crash_recovery_seen: 42` can't crash the post-mount recovery scan. Forward-compat unknown-key passthrough already handled it but the schema is the documented contract for "what's persisted."

* **`BulkExportCollectionModal` added to `tests/test_modal_boundaries.py::_MODAL_CASES`.** The 160×48 fit guarantee now covers the new collection-export modal.

---

## [INV-65] Sweep #24 — custom enzymes + enzyme collections hardening (2026-05-22)
Audit-driven cleanup for the in-flight "custom enzymes + enzyme collections" feature (~2000 LOC added before v1.0.0). The new `_save_*` helpers landed but the cross-cutting contracts ([INV-50] `_dismiss_once`, [INV-51] save-failure notify, [INV-64] `_AGENT_BACKUP_LABELS` parity, the `_NEB_ENZYMES`-vs-`_all_enzymes()` consumer audit) were not all wired through. New tests: 13 cases in `tests/test_enzyme_collections.py` + 9 cases in `tests/test_new_agent_endpoints.py`.

* **`_AGENT_BACKUP_LABELS` parity (P0 ship-blocker).** `_USER_DATA_FILE_ATTRS` got `_CUSTOM_ENZYMES_FILE` + `_ENZYME_COLLECTIONS_FILE` and `RestoreFromBackupModal._TARGETS` covered both, but `_AGENT_BACKUP_LABELS` (`splicecraft.py:73145-73166`) was missed — the agent restore-backup endpoint couldn't list/restore the two new files. The `_h_restore_backup` cache-bust map and the bulk-restore cache tuple in `_h_restore_pre_update_snapshot` had the same gap. All three maps now mirror `_USER_DATA_FILE_ATTRS` minus `_SETTINGS_FILE`. Regression guard: existing `tests/test_sweep10.py::test_agent_backup_labels_parity_with_user_data_files` + new `test_agent_backup_labels_include_enzyme_files` in `tests/test_enzyme_collections.py`.

* **Modal `_dismiss_once` retrofit ([INV-50]).** `AddCustomEnzymeModal`, `EnzymeCollectionsModal`, and `_EnzymeNamePromptModal` all write to disk on dismiss (or carry payloads that trigger downstream saves) but lacked the double-fire guard. A double-click on Save in `AddCustomEnzymeModal` appended the same custom enzyme twice; the second pass bypassed the duplicate-name check because the FIRST pass had already persisted. Pattern uniform across all three: `_dismissed: bool = False` in `__init__` (AFTER docstring per [INV-41]), `_dismiss_once(payload)` helper, every exit path (button + Esc + worker callback) routed through it.

* **`_save_or_status` modal helper.** `EnzymeCollectionsModal` had six bare `_save_enzyme_collections(entries)` callsites in the CRUD button handlers (New / Rename / Duplicate / Delete / Add / Remove). Sacred invariant #7: `_safe_save_json` re-raises on OSError; without a wrap the exception would crash the modal mid-CRUD instead of routing through `_notify_save_failure`. New `EnzymeCollectionsModal._save_or_status(entries) -> bool` helper handles try/except + `_notify_save_failure` + inline `_set_status` red toast; each callsite becomes `if not self._save_or_status(entries): return`. Mirrors the pattern from `AddCustomEnzymeModal._save_btn` which wraps correctly.

* **Scan/cloning path routing to `_all_enzymes()` (P0).** Custom enzymes are visible to the restriction-overlay scan via `_SCAN_CATALOG` (rebuilt from `_all_enzymes()` after every `_save_custom_enzymes`), but **seven downstream consumers still keyed on `_NEB_ENZYMES` directly** — a user could add `MyEnzI` but couldn't use it in Domesticator, fragment excision, cloning grammars, or via the agent. Fixed:
  - `_enzyme_cuts_impl` (the underlying scanner used by all cloning paths via `_enzyme_cuts`) — local `catalog = _all_enzymes()` at function entry, all membership tests + indexing route through it.
  - `_excise_fragment_pair` (synthetic 2-enzyme digest used by traditional cloning UI) — same pattern.
  - `_site_for_enzyme` (cloning-grammar site lookup) — `_all_enzymes().get(enzyme)`.
  - `_clone_part_into_entry_vector` + IIS-conflict guard + second-pass clone helper (3 sites) — grammar's enzyme can now be a custom one.
  - `_design_cloning_primers` (cloning primer design) — local `catalog = _all_enzymes()`.
  - `_settings_validator_custom_enzymes_csv` (legacy CSV settings validator).
  - `_h_list_restriction_sites` agent endpoint — new `respect_active_collection: bool = true` parameter; when no explicit `enzymes` list is provided, defaults to the user's active enzyme collection so the agent mirrors UI overlay semantics. Explicit `enzymes` always wins.

* **`(IndexError|NoMatches, Exception)` antipattern cleanup.** 7 sites used the redundant tuple-with-Exception pattern that [INV-64] flagged as bare-except in disguise. `HistoryViewerModal` (19586) + `HistoryScreen` (19799) narrowed to `except IndexError:`. 5 sites in `ExperimentEditor` / `SequencingScreen` / `action_layout_*` narrowed to `except NoMatches:`. Sacred lesson: `Exception` already subsumes `IndexError` / `NoMatches`, so the tuple form is the same as `except Exception` — pyright won't catch it, only grep will.

* **12 new agent endpoints (CRUD parity per [INV-64] sweep #23 pattern).**
  - **Custom enzymes:** `list-custom-enzymes`, `get-custom-enzyme`, `create-custom-enzyme`, `update-custom-enzyme`, `delete-custom-enzyme`. Built-in NEB names refused for create (409) — agent must add a custom enzyme with the SAME name to override (matches the `_all_enzymes()` override-by-name contract). Shared `_agent_validate_custom_enzyme_payload` helper mirrors `AddCustomEnzymeModal._validate` so UI + agent accept the same shape.
  - **Enzyme collections:** `list-enzyme-collections`, `get-enzyme-collection`, `create-enzyme-collection`, `update-enzyme-collection`, `delete-enzyme-collection`. Update supports rename via `new_name` + enzyme-list replacement; rename onto an existing name returns 409. Delete clears the active-pointer if the deleted collection was active. Unknown enzyme names accepted at create/update time — `_active_enzyme_allowed_set` filters them at scan time so a custom enzyme added later participates retroactively.
  - **Active pointer:** `get-active-enzyme-collection`, `set-active-enzyme-collection`. `set` refuses unknown names (404); pass `name: null` to clear.

* **`_save_custom_enzymes` cache invalidation (correctness, not perf).** After `_save_custom_enzymes`, `_SCAN_CATALOG` is rebuilt, but three other caches that key on enzyme NAMES (not definitions) held stale results: `_ENZYME_CUTS_CACHE` (LRU on `(hash(seq), tuple(sorted enzymes), circular)`), `_RESTR_SCAN_CACHE` (LRU on the scan params), and `_ASSEMBLY_FRAGMENT_CACHE` (which caches `None` for unresolved enzymes — a newly-added custom enzyme would forever hit the cached `None`). All three now `.clear()` at the end of `_save_custom_enzymes`. `_save_enzyme_collections` does NOT need this fix — `_RESTR_SCAN_CACHE`'s key already includes `allowed_enzymes`, so it self-invalidates on collection change.

* **`AddCustomEnzymeModal` agent-payload validation hardening.** Pyright caught `int(payload.get("fwd_cut"))` accepting `None` and raising at runtime via `TypeError`. Refactored to extract the raw values, explicit `None` check ("missing 'fwd_cut' or 'rev_cut'" → 400), then `int(raw)` inside a `(TypeError, ValueError)` catch — cleaner error message + appeases pyright.

* **`_EnzymeNamePromptModal` fit guarantee.** Added to `tests/test_modal_boundaries.py::_MODAL_CASES` so the 160×48 baseline-terminal fit test exercises the prompt modal alongside `EnzymeCollectionsModal` + `AddCustomEnzymeModal` + `SettingsModal` (all already covered from initial landing).

* **Deferred (P2 follow-up candidates).** UI palette dropdowns (`_CLONING_RE_OPTIONS` at 32355, restriction-insert modal at 51199, traditional-cloning enzyme picker at 55246, primer-design dialog at 62776) still enumerate `_NEB_ENZYMES.keys()` directly. Custom enzymes appear in the scan + cloning paths but NOT in these dropdown widgets — a user can type a custom-enzyme name in a custom field but can't pick from the picker. Lower priority since the cloning path resolution is correct; UX gap, not a robustness gap. `EditGrammarDialog` enzyme validation (38808) similarly accepts only NEB enzymes — defer until a user requests custom-enzyme-keyed grammars. Library-walk perf items from audit #3 (BulkExportCollectionModal compose, full-library entry-id lookup at 3 sites) deferred — they're optimisations not correctness fixes, and benchmarking them is more cost than the 1.0.0 deadline allows.

## [INV-66] Sweep #25 — full adversarial audit (2026-05-23)
Four parallel sub-agents covered footguns/correctness, performance, resource utilization, and hardening; each briefed with INV-36…INV-65 to avoid duplicating prior sweeps. Combined surfaced ~40 findings; this sweep landed all CRITICAL/HIGH and most MEDIUM/LOW. New tests: 37 cases in `tests/test_sweep25.py`. Several MEDIUM items (M7 export TOCTOU via O_EXCL, M8 export-endpoint symlink ancestor walk, M12 clipboard-dir retention, M15/M16/M17/M18/M19 additional iterator-conversion + cache caps, M21 stale-canvas guard on Plasmidsaurus alignment, H13 `_draw_linear_flag` wrap-feat enumerate, H7 fd-pass into zipfile) and LOW items (L2/L4/L5/L7/L8/L10) were assessed lower-value or larger-than-sweep and deferred to a future sweep.

* **Cache-bust enumeration drift CLOSED (P0).** `_MASTER_DELETE_CACHE_ATTRS` is now the single source of truth for both restore paths (`RestoreFromBackupModal._do_restore` + `_h_restore_pre_update_snapshot`). Pre-sweep both maintained their own hand-listed tuples that drifted: sweep #9 added experiments/projects/gels (missed in modal); sweep #20 added `_primer_usage_cache` + `_feature_library_index_cache` (missed in both); sweep #24 added `_custom_enzymes_cache` + `_enzyme_collections_cache` (missed in modal). Future caches now enroll automatically. `RestoreFromBackupModal` continues to skip `_settings_cache` (intentional — UI restore would clobber live pointer state mid-session); the agent path includes it (invoked outside the running app). Regression guards in `TestCacheBustParity`.

* **`_iter_library_readonly` + `_iter_parts_bin_readonly` + `_find_library_entry_by_name` helpers.** Mirror the sweep-#23 `_iter_collections_readonly` pattern: read-only view returns cached list directly (no `_typed_clone`). Pre-sweep ~40 library-iteration sites paid the full multi-MB deepcopy on every call (every primer-usage scan, every plasmid pick, every collection sync). Converted 13 highest-impact sites in this sweep: agent endpoints (`_h_list_library`, `_h_list_collections`), id-set construction for save-time disambiguation (4 sites), primer-usage scanners (`_index_primer_usage_in_collections` + `_find_primer_plasmid_usages`), assembly fragment recover, single-id agent endpoints (`_h_transfer_annotations`, `_h_diff_plasmid`, `_h_align_plasmidsaurus_zip` — id+name via new sibling helper), and `LibraryPanel._row_selected`. Remaining ~70 callsites (mostly write-paths that legitimately need `_load_library()`) audited as safe-as-is.

* **Lock-discipline gap widened (`_cache_lock`).** INV-50/51 closed cross-group worker RMW races for Constructor/Gibson/Domesticator/Traditional; this sweep extended:
  - **Custom-enzyme + enzyme-collection agent endpoints** (`_h_create_custom_enzyme`, `_h_update_custom_enzyme`, `_h_delete_custom_enzyme`, `_h_create_enzyme_collection`, `_h_update_enzyme_collection`, `_h_delete_enzyme_collection`) — full collision-check + load + mutate + save under `_cache_lock`. INV-65 added these endpoints but missed the lock; two concurrent agent calls could both pass the collision check then both append, silently dropping the first writer.
  - **App-level Ctrl+S `_save_worker` library RMW** (the canonical save path that the lock was originally built for!). Pre-fix `_load_library()` happened outside the lock; a Gibson/Constructor save landing between load and `_save_library` would be silently dropped.
  - **Five disk-writer workers** (`_add_save_to_disk`, `_delete_save_to_disk`, `_collections_save_to_disk`, `_trad_save_to_disk`, `_rename_save_to_disk`) now wrap their `_safe_save_json` calls in `with _cache_lock:`. Pre-fix cross-group workers could race the disk writes. The current design (UI thread updates cache; worker writes disk) is preserved — the lock only serialises the writes. Remaining race: cross-group out-of-order writes can still land a stale payload over a newer one; mitigation deferred to a future sweep (clean fix is "worker writes the latest cache state, not the dispatch-time payload" — bigger refactor).
  - **`_sync_active_collection_plasmids` sync path** — full RMW under `_cache_lock`, using `_iter_collections_readonly` + targeted `_typed_clone` of only the modified entry (pre-fix paid 2× ~160 MB deepcopies per save on main thread; now reads by reference, clones only the target).

* **`_SCAN_CATALOG` atomic global reassign.** `_rebuild_scan_catalog` (called after every `_save_custom_enzymes` per INV-65) was clearing+appending the global list in-place. A concurrent `_restr_scan_worker` iterating the list could see a half-built catalog → missed cut sites or `IndexError` mid-iteration (visible as flickering restriction overlay mid-enzyme-add). Now builds local list then `globals()["_SCAN_CATALOG"] = new_list` (atomic GIL-protected).

* **Agent server hardening (H5/H8/H9/M4/M5/M6).** Pre-sweep `if write and not self._check_token()` — read endpoints were unauthenticated, despite several doing real work (hmmscan, blast, list-library, zip parse) that leaks filesystem state or consumes CPU/RAM. Now:
  - **All endpoints require bearer token** (except `""` / `tools` which short-circuit for client self-describe before token-acquisition).
  - **Token check fires BEFORE handler lookup** so unknown paths uniformly 401 (don't leak endpoint-list oracle via 404).
  - **Write endpoints require POST** (`GET /delete-from-library` pasted into a browser no longer silently fires); read endpoints accept both GET/POST (queries via body stay ergonomic).
  - **`_AgentAPIServer.timeout = 30` + handler `timeout = 30` + `request_queue_size = 16`** for slow-loris mitigation.
  - **`_read_body` returns `"__bad_body__"` sentinel** on malformed JSON / UTF-8 errors → dispatcher converts to 400 with `"malformed JSON body"`. Pre-fix silent `{}` masked caller serialisation bugs.
  - **Exception fall-through scrubs `_DATA_DIR` paths from response body** via `_scrub_path`. Pre-fix `OSError`/`FileNotFoundError`/`PermissionError` `str(exc)` carried the full offending path → leaked data-dir layout.
  - **`_h_load_file` + `_h_list_plasmidsaurus_members` + `_h_align_plasmidsaurus_zip` errors collapsed to uniform 400** with `"file rejected (see splicecraft log)"`. Pre-fix differentiated 404/400/413 responses (with size info) acted as a filesystem-state oracle for unauthenticated callers. Logs still carry the local-user diagnostic detail.
  - **`_h_gibson_assemble`** now wraps `_agent_refresh_library_panel(app)` in `app.call_from_thread(...)` — every other library-mutating endpoint had this; Gibson was the one site missed. Pre-fix touched DataTable widgets from the HTTP worker thread.
  - **Token via `secrets.token_urlsafe(32)`** (256-bit) — was `uuid.uuid4().hex` (122-bit). `secrets` is the stdlib's documented bearer-token primitive.

* **`_get_setting` cache-direct read.** 59 callsites; pre-fix every call did `_load_settings().get(key, default)` which fully deepcopies the settings dict. `EditCustomizeModal` open paid 4 clones; `_get_active_collection_name()` paid 24. Now reads `_settings_cache.get(key, default)` directly under `_cache_lock`, `_typed_clone`s only the matched value (negligible cost for the JSON primitives that cover ~99% of keys; still defensive for the rare container values like `experiments_custom_dict`).

* **`_rc(tgt_seq)` hoist in `_find_annotation_transfers`.** Pre-fix the RC call was inside the per-feature loop, paying O(F × N_tgt) — for a 100 kb source × 200 features, ~20 MB of RC work that should be ~100 kb. Now hoisted above the loop.

* **`_GB_PARSE_CACHE` cap 64 → 16.** A parsed SeqRecord is 5–15× its source text in memory; at 64 × 64 MB worst case the cache could hold ~40 GB resident (no byte-aware eviction). 16 entries still covers observed hot paths.

* **`_STACKS_LOG_FD` startup-time rotation.** `faulthandler.register` holds a borrowed FD so we can't rotate mid-session; truncate at startup if prior sessions accumulated > 10 MB. Pre-fix unbounded growth (a user hitting SIGUSR1 once a week accumulated MBs over a year).

* **`_safe_identifier` validator on `active_*` settings.** A hand-edited `settings.json` with `"active_project": "../../../etc/foo"` passed `_validate_settings` because only string-type was checked. Currently looked up by name (safe), but a future refactor joining the name into a path would silently inherit a traversal vuln. New `_is_safe_identifier` helper rejects `..`, `/`, `\\`, NUL on the 6 `active_*` keys.

* **Snapshot directory-restore 5 GB cap.** Pre-fix the file branch of `_restore_pre_update_snapshot` had mandatory sha256 verify; the directory branch (`shutil.copytree`) had no size check. Tampered manifest could declare `n_files=0` for a multi-GB dir and exhaust user disk.

* **`_VECTOR_MATCH_CACHE` key on `hash(gb_text)` not raw string.** Pre-fix 64 × 5 MB worst case = 320 MB just in tuple keys. `hash(str)` is deterministic per-process; collisions functionally indistinguishable from cache miss.

* **GFF3 silent feature drop now logs.** `_gff3_features_to_biopython` drops out-of-range or zero-length features; pre-fix the drop was silent (one bad row in a multi-part feature destroyed the entire feature with no warning). Now `_log.info` per drop with the `gff_id`.

* **`RestoreFromBackupModal._dismiss_once` retrofit.** INV-64 covered other writes-on-dismiss modals; this one was missed. Button + Esc race could double-fire callback.

* **Bare-except narrowing.** `except (OSError, Exception)` → `(OSError, RuntimeError, ValueError, KeyError, TypeError)` in `_h_design_primers`'s Primer3 catch (INV-65's grep for `(AttributeError, Exception)` shape missed `(OSError, Exception)`). NCBI/Kazusa network fetches → `(OSError, _urllib_error.URLError [, ET.ParseError])`. `target_seq = ""` alignment fallback → `(AttributeError, TypeError)`. Sentinel test added in `TestBareExceptNarrowing`.

* **`AddCustomEnzymeModal._save_btn` wrapped properly.** INV-65 said "wraps correctly" but only the in-modal status line was wrapped, not the structured event. Now uses `_cache_lock` for the full RMW AND calls `_notify_save_failure` so failures emit the `save.failed` structured event Alt+D bug bundles pick up.

* **Crash-recovery prune (30-day age + 50-file cap).** Pre-fix `_CRASH_RECOVERY_DIR` accumulated forever; a user hitting one crash per week for a year accumulated ~50 unnoticed `.gb` files (each a full plasmid dump, ~10–500 KB). Prune fires on every `_check_crash_recovery` startup pass.

* **`_atomic_copy` / `_atomic_marker_write` / agent token file write fd-close.** Pre-fix `open(src, "rb")` or `os.fdopen` raising before the `with` construct leaked `fd`. New `fd_owned` flag tracks ownership; except path closes the raw fd if `_os.fdopen` never took it.

* **`splicecraft_cli._read_session` lstat token file.** Pre-fix `f.stat()` followed symlinks; a pre-placed symlink at `~/.local/share/splicecraft/agent_token` pointing to `/etc/passwd` would be silently followed and leak first 1 KB via the malformed-port stderr error. Now `lstat` + `S_ISLNK` refusal.

* **Lockfile unlink on graceful exit.** `_release_data_dir_lock` now best-effort-unlinks `splicecraft.lock` after the `LOCK_UN`. Cosmetic on local filesystems (PID-alive recheck handles stale lockfiles), but cleaner on NFS/SMB shares.

* **Architectural recommendation — LANDED (2026-07-03).** The recurring cache-bust enumeration drift (caught 4 times: INV-43, INV-50, INV-64, here) and `_AGENT_BACKUP_LABELS` parity drift (INV-64) suggested a `_PERSISTENT_CACHE_REGISTRY` with `label` / `file_attr` / `cache_attr` / `user_data` / `master_delete`. Now shipped in `splicecraft_backup.py`: one canonical `_CacheSpec` table (23 entries — 21 user-data files + 2 index-only caches) that every parallel enumeration (`_USER_DATA_FILE_ATTRS`, `_MASTER_DELETE_CACHE_ATTRS`, `_AGENT_BACKUP_LABELS`, the conftest sandbox pairs) is a documented VIEW of. **Deliberately NOT a runtime rewire** — the backup / wipe / master-delete enumeration stays explicit + auditable (SACRED data-safety code); instead the `test_registry_*` cases in `tests/test_data_safety.py` (class `TestRealFilesNeverTouched`, 5 cases) assert each runtime list matches the registry EXACTLY, so a future addition that drifts any view fails loudly and is named. Add a cache to the registry → the test tells you precisely which lists still need it. Kills the drift bug class without touching the data-safety runtime.

### Sweep #26 follow-up (same INV-66; 2026-05-23 same-day)

User asked for "fix all deferred" after sweep #25's first pass landed v0.9.20. This second pass closed every deferred-list item with concrete value, leaving only the v1.1 architectural refactor unaddressed. Test count grew 37 → 61 in `tests/test_sweep25.py`; the file's docstring documents both batches.

* **L2 — PyPI HTTPS-by-default.** `_resolve_pypi_url` now refuses `http://` overrides unless `SPLICECRAFT_PYPI_INSECURE=1` is explicitly set. The update-check response feeds version-comparison logic; an in-path attacker on a corporate LAN could downgrade-attack via plain-http manipulation. Tests in `TestSweep26PypiHttpDowngrade`.

* **L4 — `_check_data_files` driven by `_USER_DATA_FILE_ATTRS` registry.** Pre-fix the 17-entry list was hand-maintained — same parity-drift class as INV-64 caught for `_AGENT_BACKUP_LABELS`. Now derives labels from `_AGENT_BACKUP_LABELS` inversion (with special-case overrides for 3 non-standard hand-labels) plus an attr-name fallback. New files added to `_USER_DATA_FILE_ATTRS` auto-enroll in startup validation. Tests in `TestSweep26CheckDataFilesRegistry`.

* **L5 — `_ENZYME_CUT_RANGE = 30` module constant.** Replaces hardcoded `±30` in `AddCustomEnzymeModal._validate` (UI) and `_agent_validate_custom_enzyme_payload` (agent). Single point of adjustment. Tests in `TestSweep26EnzymeCutRangeConstant`.

* **L6 — NCBI / Kazusa narrow excepts use `urllib.error.URLError`.** `_ncbi_taxid_search` and `_codon_fetch_kazusa` now `import urllib.error as _urllib_error` and catch `(OSError, _urllib_error.URLError [, ET.ParseError])` instead of bare `Exception`. Real bugs in the parse paths (KeyError on missing XML field, etc.) now propagate instead of disguising as "Network error".

* **L7 — `agent.write.ok` log fires AFTER `_send` succeeds.** A broken-socket `_send` failure now emits a distinct `agent.write.send_failed` event so forensics can distinguish "handler succeeded but client never saw the response" from "handler succeeded and response delivered". Pre-sweep the log fired before the response landed; a `_send` failure could leave the audit log saying success while the client retried. Tests in `TestSweep26LogEventTimingAfterSend`.

* **L8 — `_ACCEPTOR_TU_PAIRS_CACHE` FIFO cap = 64.** Pre-sweep unbounded (only invalidated on `_save_entry_vectors`). Bounded in practice by `(grammar_id × enzyme)` tuples, but a user with many custom grammars could grow this. Mirrors `_VECTOR_MATCH_CACHE`'s 64-entry cap.

* **L9 — already landed in sweep #25's first pass** (lockfile unlink on graceful exit).

* **L10 — `_build_system_info` rglob cap at 10k files.** Pre-sweep unbounded `rglob('*')` count over user-data dirs. On heavy installs (thousands of attachments / crash recovery files / .dna sidecars) this was a CPU burst per diagnostic bundle / log-startup. The exact count past 10k isn't useful for triage — `"≥10000"` tells the same story.

* **M3 — already landed in sweep #25** (snapshot dir restore 5 GB cap).

* **M7 — assessed already-mitigated.** Audit suggested O_EXCL on export writes. `_atomic_write_text`'s inner `lstat` check catches symlink-mid-flight; the remaining "regular file created between agent's check and atomic-replace" race isn't a security gap (just a different overwrite than expected). Skipped intentionally.

* **M8 — `_check_agent_read_path_ancestors` helper + applied to Plasmidsaurus paths.** New helper mirrors `_check_agent_write_path`'s ancestor-walk pattern for read endpoints. Applied to `_h_list_plasmidsaurus_members` + `_h_align_plasmidsaurus_zip` so a parent symlink (`~/Documents` → `/etc`) doesn't silently redirect the downstream `os.open`. Tests in `TestSweep26AncestorReadCheck`.

* **M12 — clipboard fallback dir prune.** `_DATA_DIR/clipboard/` tier-3 tmpfiles now prune on every write: 7-day age cap OR 100-file count cap (oldest first). Pre-sweep the dir accumulated forever; a long-running session that frequently hit tier-3 (broken X11 / SSH / WSL-no-tty) could leave hundreds of MB of sequence-shaped tmpfiles around.

* **M16 — `ConstructorModal._build_palette_rows` + 5 more parts-bin readers** use `_iter_parts_bin_readonly`. Loop bodies only `.get()` fields then build fresh row dicts.

* **M17 — `_iter_all_grammars_readonly` helper added.** Skips the per-call `deepcopy` of every built-in grammar dict that `_all_grammars` pays. Built-ins returned by reference (callers MUST NOT mutate); custom grammars get a shallow clone for `editable=True` injection. Callsite conversions deferred to a future sweep — adding the helper is the gating change. Tests in `TestSweep26GrammarsReadonly`.

* **M18 — `_gb_text_to_record(cache=False)` for batch parses.** New keyword arg lets one-shot batch consumers skip the `_GB_PARSE_CACHE` populate. Used in Plasmidsaurus `_batch_extract_gbk_meta` where 50+ samples × multi-MB assemblies would absorb the entire batch into the cache (~250 MB pressure in one click). Tests in `TestSweep26GBParseCacheFlag`.

* **M15 — 4 more library-iter conversions** (entry-vector picker, linear-library picker, traditional-cloning lib-by-name index, add-record id-set). All read-only patterns, no mutation.

* **M19 — assessed lower-value.** Per-cache count cap of 16 is a reasonable bound; aggregate-bytes tracking adds complexity for a worst-case scenario that requires multiple multi-MB plasmids open in series. Documented as-is.

* **M21 — `_h_align_plasmidsaurus_zip` post-alignment target-drift detection.** Captures `resolved_target_id` at handler entry; after the (possibly multi-second) `_pairwise_align` completes, re-looks up by id. Returns 410 Gone if the target was deleted mid-flight (alignment result is technically valid but the named target no longer exists for downstream `set-active`/`load-entry`). Surfaces `_target_renamed_to` flag in the result payload if the target was renamed (alignment still ships; agent's follow-ups use the current name). Tests in `TestSweep26AlignmentDriftDetection`.

* **H7 — TOCTOU-safe zip open via `os.open` + `fileobj=`.** Both `_list_gbk_members_in_zip` and `_extract_gbk_member` now open the zip path via `os.open(path, O_RDONLY)` (which dereferences the symlink ONCE), `fstat` on the fd (immune to a concurrent path swap), then `zipfile.ZipFile(fileobj=os.fdopen(...))`. Closes the TOCTOU window that pre-sweep let a hostile local process swap the file between the path-based stat and the path-based `ZipFile(str(p))`. Explicit `fobj.close()` in `finally` since `ZipFile.close()` doesn't close caller-supplied fileobj. Tests in `TestSweep26ZipFdPass`.

* **H13 — `_wrap_feats_idx` precomputed in `load_record`.** `_draw_linear_flag` no longer enumerates every feature on every render frame just to filter for wrap features. Maintains `_wrap_feats_idx: list[int]` alongside `_feats_by_start` per the established precompute pattern. On dense plasmids (1000+ features) the per-frame O(F) wrap scan was a noticeable tax on rotation + zoom interaction. Tests in `TestSweep26WrapFeatsIdx`.

* **Removed unused `uuid` import** (left over from sweep #25 L3 switching to `secrets.token_urlsafe(32)`). Caught by `ruff check` in `release.py`'s gate.

* **Architectural cache-registry refactor — LANDED 2026-07-03** (`_PERSISTENT_CACHE_REGISTRY` in `splicecraft_backup.py` + the `test_registry_*` guards in `test_data_safety.py`); see the INV-66 note above.
